# ROADMAP.md - NoteLib

Rebrand note: StudySnap has been rebranded to NoteLib while preserving current database schema naming unless explicitly changed.

Goal: evolve NoteLib from a one-shot generator into a reusable note-first study workspace.

## Current Release Baseline

**Kicked off 2026-09-11, signed off 2026-09-12.** `v0.143.0 — No Way Out` is **Released** — both
planned items shipped as scoped, PRs #1381 and #1382, merged into `releases/v0.143.0` at
`f715dada`/`77b6c226`. (1) `long-exam/page.tsx`'s `useExamFocusMode` now reads
`phase === "running" && !submitting`, matching Challenge Quiz's `v0.131.0` guard. (2)
`ExamQuestionPoolService.refreshPool` is now called unconditionally on every Study Pack
regeneration — both the combined and default `STUDY_PACK`-only scopes — closing leg (1) of the
two-part "derived artifacts" Backlog row; leg (2), the Challenge question bank, stays open.
**⚠️ THIS KICKOFF'S SCAN CORRECTED FOUR STALE BACKLOG ROWS BEFORE SETTLING ON THIS SCOPE**, and
item 2's own scope was widened again before its Codex prompt was written — see `RELEASES.md`'s
"How this scope was reached". **A scoped cold falsification pass on item 2 found and fixed a real
deadlock risk** (a lock-order inversion between `study_packs` and `exam_question_pool`, verified
empirically against real Postgres) **and confirmed a fourth path
(`AdminStudyPackTransactionHelper`) still bypasses the fix**, tracked as its own Backlog row, not
fixed here. Full detail in `RELEASES.md` and `docs/releases/v0.143.0.md`.

**Kicked off 2026-09-11, signed off 2026-09-11.** `v0.142.0 — Awareness Before Action` is
**Released** (PRs #1378, #1379; A5 and the notification card redesign merged directly on the
release branch). It polishes two already-shipped surfaces the owner is now actively announcing to
users: the in-app notification inbox and the adopted Review Set update panel. Source:
`docs/claude-plans/v0.142.0-adoption-and-notifications-plan.md`. **All four planned items shipped
as scoped — Option A (Subject Plan grouping only) was taken, not Option B, so the release stayed
frontend-only as designed until a pre-signoff falsification pass found a real backend race
(below), fixed inline and documented as a scope note in `RELEASES.md`.**
**⚠️ THE FIRST ITEM (A5) IS A LIVE BUG ON 100% OF TODAY'S NOTIFICATION POPULATION, VERIFIED READ-ONLY
2026-09-11: all 42 production notifications share one type (`REVIEW_SET_UPDATE`), all 42 carry a
CTA, and the CTA `<Link>` at `notification-inbox.tsx:163-169` marks read but never closes the
panel — every notification in production is one tap from this exact defect, and 41 of 42 are
still unread.** **⚠️ THE OWNER'S STATED REASON FOR THE CARD REDESIGN WAS WRONG, THE REDESIGN ITSELF
IS NOT: borders between notifications already exist (`border-b border-border`, `:154`) and are
merely invisible with one notification on screen; what is genuinely missing is a background
distinction for unread beyond `font-medium` vs `font-semibold` (`:157`).** **SCOPE:** (1) fix A5
first, inline-sized, ship as its own small PR; (2) redesign the notification card — single tap
target, no separate CTA link or Mark-read button, dismiss stays a distinct control, a relative
timestamp (free — `createdAt` is already in `NotificationResponse`); (3) replace the Review Set
update panel's two uncapped raw-diff lists with meaning-based partitioning (`SKIPPED_NOT_PUBLIC`
currently sits under a heading that says "no action taken", which is false — it is the branch that
decides what will be skipped), aggregate the three per-note fan-out types (`REORDERED`, `RETIRED`,
`MOVED`) into counts, and move from an uncapped inline wall to a compact summary plus a
progressive-disclosure "Review update" detail surface; (4) rename **Apply additions** → **Add N
new topics**, counting `ADDED_NOTE` alone — NOT `additionsAvailable()`
(`NoteCollectionService.java:3523-3529`), which also counts `ADDED_SUBJECT_PLAN` and would
overstate the promised count. **⚠️ SECTION GROUPING IS AN OWNER DECISION DEFAULTED AT KICKOFF:
ships as Option A — Subject Plan grouping only, no Section level — to keep this release
frontend-only.** A Section is a string label on `NoteCollectionItemEntity.label`, not an entity,
and the update payload carries no Section field today; adding one (`sourceSectionLabel`, populated
from a variable already in scope at the `ADDED_NOTE` construction site — no extra query) is a real
but deferred fast-follow (Option B) that would make this a backend release and raise the
verification tier. **Revisit if the owner wants the extra hierarchy level; nothing forecloses it.**
**⚠️ THE RENAME'S ONE COLLISION RISK IS CLEARED: a read-only production check for "Apply additions" /
"upstream" / "addition" across all notification titles, bodies and CTA labels returned ZERO ROWS.**
Two in-repo references still need sweeping in the same PR: `docs/features/collections.md:932` and
`frontend/app/collections/[id]/page.test.tsx:736,758`. **⚠️ ANTI-DRIFT: do NOT let notification
activation apply an update — navigation and mark-read only, the update stays a separate explicit
action; do NOT title-infer the Subject Plan grouping — use stored `sourcePlanId`/`subjectTitle`
identity; do NOT change `additionsAvailable()`'s boolean-gate semantics; do NOT add pagination, a
new diff engine, or new notification infrastructure — Option A needs none of it; do NOT sweep
`AGENTS.md`'s preamble or the Backlog Index further as a side effect (item 6 of
`docs/claude-plans/context-doc-token-reduction-plan.md` ran ONCE at this kickoff, six rows moved —
see the Backlog Index note above — items 4/6/7 stay otherwise held).** Verification tier: **one
scoped cold agent minimum, falsification-framed** — two PRs land on the same shared classification
surface, and this release changes what a user-facing claim means (the "no action taken" heading),
the exact class that has cost three releases running when swept only by diff. **Escalates to the
full three-agent test if Option B ships instead of A.** **Routing: CODEX** for both workstreams;
A5 is inline-sized and should ship first, as its own PR. Full scope, the verified production reads,
and every rejected alternative are in the plan file above.

**Kicked off 2026-09-10, signed off 2026-09-11.** `v0.141.0 — Formulas That Render` is **Released** on `releases/v0.141.0` (PRs #1374, #1375, #1376), cut from `main` after `v0.140.0` merged as #1373 and tagged. **⚠️ THE HEADLINE IS A LIVE LEARNER-FACING DEFECT, DIAGNOSED BY A COLD PRESSURE TEST AND VERIFIED TWICE AGAINST PRODUCTION:** in a quiz question that mentions money AND a formula, the currency `$` OPENS a math span that closes on the formula's `$`, so KaTeX is handed the whole sentence, fails, and the fallback re-emits the source — **the reader sees raw LaTeX with backslashes visible.** `isInlineDollarOpen` (`quiz-working-solution.tsx:62`) opens on any `$` not followed by whitespace, so `$50,000` qualifies. **22 questions + 49 explanations affected.** **⚠️ A FIX AIMED AT `normalizeBareMath` WOULD BE A NO-OP — this session proposed exactly that and was wrong.** The repair is wired correctly at every call site and reaches nothing, because **339/339** backslash-bearing questions and **498/498** explanations already carry a delimiter, so it early-returns. **SCOPE:** (1) the currency/formula mis-pairing; (2) `SummaryMarkdown` never normalises bare math (36 summaries carry a backslash and NO delimiter); (3) `app/shared/study-packs/[id]/page.tsx:69,75,81` render summary, keyConcepts and fullNotes as raw `{value}` with no math rendering at all. **⚠️ ITEM 3 IS SCOPED HONESTLY AND MUST NOT BE OVERSOLD: that route has 1 linked-learner relationship and 6 share events in 90 days, and `share_token` is 0 across all 7,573 packs.** Three surfaces, so signoff owes **one scoped cold agent**.

**Kicked off 2026-09-10, signed off 2026-09-10.** `v0.140.0 — Pending Work in Reach` is **Released** on `releases/v0.140.0` (PRs #1370, #1371, #1372), cut from `main` after `v0.139.0` merged and tagged. Source: `docs/claude-plans/authoring-and-quiz-legibility-fix-plan.md` §§5-7 and **§10** — owner-reported 2026-09-05 from real use. **⚠️ A LEGIBILITY FIX, NOT A DATA-LOSS DEFECT:** navigation protection already exists (`study-plan-builder-page-client.tsx:1663-1671`, verified at kickoff), so work is not silently lost; the failure is a curator dragging on a long plan, scrolling away from the header holding Save, and redoing the arrangement. **Trigger verified live — a 79-note plan, two at 77, one at 76, five more at 59+; 12 authors created 1,768 notes in the 14 days to 2026-09-10.** Scope: the dirty-state sticky bar; `[DECISION]` copy wording owed from the owner (both obvious wordings are wrong in opposite directions, §10 Finding B); §7 narrowed on evidence (its premise is false, §10 Finding C); and three Backlog rows corrected whose work had already shipped. **⚠️ The kickoff scan found the third consecutive release in which every stale row overstated open work — and nothing updates a row when the thing it describes SHIPS, which is a missing signoff step rather than general rot.**

**Kicked off 2026-09-10, signed off 2026-09-10.** `v0.139.0 — Reopened` is **Released** on `releases/v0.139.0` (PRs #1364, #1365, #1366, #1367), cut from `main` after `v0.138.0` merged as #1363 and tagged. Source: `docs/claude-findings/2026-09-10-september-checkpoint-reads.md`, written at THIS kickoff by running the reads that came due **before** scope was proposed — the sequencing the `v0.138.0` kickoff asked for. **⚠️⚠️ THE `v0.72.0` RETENTION CHECKPOINT FIRED ITS KILL CRITERION: nine learners were shown the review-commitment prompt between 2026-08-11 and 2026-09-09, and ZERO committed; one declined.** **⚠️ THE ZERO IS REAL AND TWO INDEPENDENT INSTRUMENTS AGREE** — `COMMITTED` and `DECLINED` are emitted from the SAME LINE (`review-commitment-prompt.tsx:102`) and `DECLINED` fired once, so the call site provably executes and the other branch simply never happened; independently, `SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE cardinality(review_days) > 0` returns **0**, which rules out the analytics-delivery bias that made `v0.80.0` necessary. The pre-committed rule said the framing *"reverts to unconfirmed and is reopened rather than iterated on with further nudge tuning"*. **⚠️ OWNER OVERRIDE, RECORDED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED OVER: the owner elected to REDESIGN THE PROMPT — the nudge tuning that rule names — after being told so. A pre-committed rule quietly stepped over stops being a rule, and the next checkpoint inherits the precedent.** **⚠️ n=9 IS SMALL BUT NOT NOTHING: a ~10% true commit rate is not excluded by chance, a ≥30% one effectively is. The redesign is a BET, not a correction, and it owes a checkpoint with a real denominator.** **SCOPE:** (1) **instrumentation FIRST, NOT optional** — the prompt fires only after a successful save (`review-commitment-prompt.tsx:96-107`), so **8 of 9 learners vanished with nothing recorded** and the funnel cannot tell *ignored* from *considered and rejected*; and all 11 `DUE_CONCEPTS_DIGEST_LANDED` rows carry a **NULL `user_id`**, which made the checkpoint's second metric — *digest → first answer AMONG COMMITTERS* — **never computable**; (2) **redesign the ask** — **⚠️ the binding constraint is the TRIGGER, not the copy: `isFirstCompletedSessionEver === true` means ONE impression per learner EVER**, asking for a weekday multi-select plus an exam date immediately after a first session, before any payoff is visible. Nine impressions in a month is a narrow trigger, not weak copy; (3) **`scripts/check-deploys.sh` must report a confirmed drift AS drift** — **VERIFIED empirically against today's live miss:** it prints *"VERCEL: serving 98ef1955 … BEHIND"* and then **exits 2**, which its own contract defines as *"could not check"*; `drift=1` at `:63` is discarded by the `exit 2` at `:82`, so `/signoff` or any CI job reading the exit code sees *"I could not look"* when the truth is *"Vercel is definitively behind"*. **⚠️⚠️ CORRECTED 2026-09-10, SAME DAY: VERCEL DID NOT MISS `v0.138.0`. It deployed `05c367c4` at 01:21:06Z, **4 minutes 24 seconds after the 01:16:42Z merge** — it was IN FLIGHT when this session checked, and the check was read as an absence. **The true record is ONE confirmed miss (`v0.136.0`), not two of three: `v0.137.0` and `v0.138.0` both auto-deployed normally.** ⚠️ **THIS IS `v0.137.0`'s OWN RULE BROKEN THE DAY AFTER IT WAS WRITTEN** — a production-state reading taken at one instant and asserted as a standing property. **⚠️ AND IT IS A REAL LESSON FOR THE DETECTOR, NOT JUST AN EMBARRASSMENT: testing for ABSENCE requires waiting past the normal deploy latency, or the test manufactures its own false positive.** Observed latency is ~2–5 minutes on both platforms. **Item 3's defect is UNAFFECTED and still real** — it was reproduced by mutation against the pre-fix script, independently of any live drift.**  Render was live at 01:18:59Z and Vercel at 01:21:06Z. **⚠️ THE CONSEQUENCE WAS COSMETIC THIS TIME AND THAT IS LUCK, NOT DESIGN: `v0.138.0` changed no controller and no `lib/api` file, so there is no API-form skew of the kind that killed Your Impact in `v0.136.0`.** **⚠️ The upstream cause is transient GitHub push-event delivery loss to the Vercel GitHub App, is INFERRED, and is NOT fixable from this repo — this release fixes the REPORTING and must say so.** **⚠️ ANTI-DRIFT: do NOT ship item 2 without item 1 (a redesign that cannot be measured reproduces this exact position); do NOT re-date the `v0.72.0` checkpoint as though it had not fired; do NOT claim the Vercel auto-deploy is fixed; do NOT make `/actuator/metrics` public to make a checkpoint readable — the `v0.134.0` row's "externally readable" claim is WRONG and `application.yaml`'s own comment says so, correct the ROW not the security posture; do NOT add an analytics event without a fire site (`v0.116.0`/`v0.117.0`); no Learning Connections work (`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]`, denominator ONE); no `frontend/app/onboarding` work before the `2026-09-11` read.** **⚠️ GUARDS: assert the dismiss event fires on the CLOSE path specifically (a test asserting "some event fires" passes today); assert the PERSISTED digest row carries a non-null `user_id`, not that the client sent one; assert EXIT CODE 1 — the message already prints, the exit code is the whole bug.** **⚠️ INHERITED: the four `FAILED` notes from `v0.138.0` are the OWNER's to re-run, and the backend went live 01:18:59Z so that `deploy + 2 days` clock has started.** **Routing: CODEX for items 1–2** (frontend across five call sites plus a backend analytics change, more than five files); **CLAUDE CODE inline for item 3** (one shell script, one exit path). Full scope and pre-declared guards in `RELEASES.md`.

**Kicked off 2026-09-09, signed off 2026-09-10.** `v0.138.0 — Stated and Enforced` is **Released** on `releases/v0.138.0` (PRs #1361, #1362), cut from `main` after `v0.137.0` merged as #1360 and tagged. Sources: `docs/claude-findings/2026-09-09-regeneration-invalid-title-failures.md` and `docs/claude-plans/2026-09-09-generated-title-bound-and-failure-observability-plan.md`, both surfaced by THIS kickoff's step 8 scan and indexed in this same commit. **⚠️⚠️ A LIVE PRODUCTION DEFECT WITH FOUR NOTES STUCK `FAILED`, AND IT IS DETERMINISTIC: batch `6a7a8fa9` retried the three failures of `f5c690f5` and all three failed identically, so retrying is not a workaround.** Seven `LLM_INVALID_OUTPUT` failures, owner-reported 2026-09-09. **The probable cause is a bound the model is never told: the title is validated at 12 WORDS while the published contract carries NO numeric bound at all — schema `maxLength: 160` CHARACTERS plus prose "concise and specific".** The same prompt file already templates `{MAX_ITEM_CHARS}` and `{MAX_WORDS}`. **⚠️ AND THE CODEBASE DOCUMENTS THIS ANTI-PATTERN AGAINST ITSELF at `OpenAiLlmStudyPackService:2553-2561`, which removed an unpublished word ceiling from BULLETS and instructs "do not reintroduce a bound the model cannot see." The title kept its.** **⚠️⚠️ BUT THE FAILING BRANCH IS INFERRED, NOT CONFIRMED — the log records neither the rejected title, its word count, nor which bound failed, so four notes failed seven times and produced zero evidence of what was wrong. That is why Leg A comes first. ⚠️ The source-title length correlation is NOT discriminating: the FAILED titles average 8.5 words, well inside the cap.** SCOPE: (1) **Leg A** — log the field, the failing bound, the measured count, the limits and the **value TRUNCATED** (owner decision); **independently shippable and ships even if Leg B slips**; (2) **Leg B1** — publish `{MAX_TITLE_WORDS}` into the prompt (owner decision; **B2 and B3 are OUT of scope and B1+B2 together is forbidden — two bounds on one field is how this defect was created**); (3) **verify the 16 scope-eligible Backlog rows against real code**; (4) extend `v0.137.0`'s rule to SHIPPED-CODE claims. **⚠️⚠️ ITEM 3 EXISTS BECAUSE THREE STALE ROWS SURFACED BY ACCIDENT IN ONE DAY, AND THE THIRD WAS OFFERED TO THE OWNER AS A `v0.137.0` RELEASE CANDIDATE: the Study Plan Builder reorder reads NOT SHIPPED but shipped in `v0.96.0` (`185e0cc7`, 2026-08-29), 41 releases ago — the `Save order` button is at `study-plan-builder-page-client.tsx:2513`.** Had it been picked, a Codex prompt would have been written to build something that exists. **A stale Backlog row costs a whole RELEASE, because this Index is the input to every kickoff's scope decision.** **⚠️ ANTI-DRIFT: do NOT raise `MAX_INVALID_OUTPUT_ATTEMPTS` (the failure is deterministic); do NOT soften title validation on the regeneration path only (the title is the note body's FIRST LINE); do NOT retitle the four failed notes (`v0.120.0` made the typed title canonical); do NOT bundle the `OfficialChallengeQuizTemplateService` seed failures; nothing may make `generateStudyPackFromExistingNoteAsync` public or route it through the `@Transactional` proxy (`v0.112.0`); no `frontend/app/onboarding` work before the `2026-09-11` read; no Learning Connections work.** **⚠️ THE FOUR FAILED NOTES ARE NOT REPAIRED BY THIS RELEASE — the re-run is the OWNER's, and it is also the only real end-to-end confirmation, since the branch could not be confirmed from logs.** **⚠️ SIZE: the owner took BOTH halves after being told size is the biggest lever on verification cost. The consequence is mild ONLY because items 3–4 change no code — the code diff stays at two files, so the tier is one `advisor()` call plus the rendered-prompt guard. It would NOT stay there under B2.** **Routing: CLAUDE CODE inline.** Full scope and pre-declared guards in `RELEASES.md`.

**Signed off 2026-09-09.** `v0.137.0 — Deploy Integrity` is **Released**, kicked off and closed the same day on `releases/v0.137.0`, cut from `main` after `v0.136.0` merged as #1359. **THREE OF FOUR PLANNED ITEMS SHIPPED; item 4 (the `2026-09-10`/`2026-09-11` reads) did NOT — the dates had not arrived, and reading early is the failure those dates exist to prevent.** Those reads are unblocked and unchanged in this index. **⚠️⚠️ THIS RELEASE OPENED AS *Deploy and Read* AND WAS RE-SCOPED THE SAME DAY BECAUSE ITS PREMISE WAS FALSE — recorded rather than overwritten, because the owner CHOSE this scope over two feature candidates on the strength of that premise.** It was justified by *"`v0.132.0` through `v0.136.0` are merged and none is deployed"*, taken from this file. **READ-ONLY PRODUCTION QUERIES DISPROVED IT: `V141` RAN 2026-09-08T12:23:16Z and `V142` RAN 2026-09-08T15:47:36Z, both `success = true`, on their own releases' auto-deploys; the profile-string write is ALREADY APPLIED (`0 old / 1 new`); and the BACKEND has auto-deployed every release all along.** Only `notifications`/`announcements` at zero rows survived the check. **⚠️⚠️ THE CHEAPEST DETECTOR EXISTED AND WAS UNUSED: the `v0.133.0` row said `V142` HAS NOT BEEN RUN in its Gate cell while its own Status cell said the read was "READ AND CLOSED 2026-09-09, one day after the deploy" — which is only possible if it HAD deployed. The Status was updated when the read closed; the Gate was not. A row that contradicts itself is free to detect.** **⚠️ THE REAL DEFECT THIS FOUND: `v0.136.0` DID NOT AUTO-DEPLOY ON EITHER PLATFORM.** Render's config is correct (`autoDeploy: yes`, trigger `commit`, branch `main`) and every prior release fired in 2–3 seconds with `trigger: new_commit`; for `4ee2c752` it never fired and the owner deployed manually. Vercel has a `vercel[bot]` Production deployment for every prior release merge and **ZERO** for this one. No repo webhooks and no CI exist, so both are GitHub Apps consuming the SAME push event and neither received it; GitHub declared no incident. **⚠️⚠️ THE RESULTING SKEW IS LIVE: Vercel still serves `7f371e65` (`v0.135.0`) while Render runs `v0.136.0`, and `v0.135.0`'s `api.ts:5990` calls `/creator-impact/me` with NO parameters from two places — which `v0.136.0` made a 400. Your Impact is broken for the owner until Vercel ships, and the change was breaking in BOTH directions (frontend-first would 404 on `/summary`), which `v0.136.0` never recorded.** SCOPE: (1) **restore the frontend deploy — OWNER ACTION**; (2) a detector for a merge that produced no deploy, **shape decided before anything is built — there is no `.github/workflows/` and this is not automatically a CI problem**; (3) two standing rules — an API form removed/renamed/made-required is breaking in BOTH directions and owes a deploy-ordering statement, and **a ROADMAP claim about PRODUCTION STATE is a snapshot, not a fact: re-read it before repeating it, and treat a Gate/Status contradiction as a defect**; (4) the reads the false premise wrongly deferred — `2026-09-10` and `2026-09-11`. **⚠️ ANTI-DRIFT: no `frontend/app/onboarding` work before the `2026-09-11` read (62.4% baseline, cannot be re-run); no Learning Connections work (`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]`, denominator ONE); do NOT change any analytics event or gating condition a live checkpoint reads; do NOT add a second `/impact` entry point before its own `deploy + 30 days` read; do NOT reach for CI as a reflex.** **⚠️ A QUIET READ IS NOT A PASS on rows carrying a denominator clause.** **⚠️ THE PRODUCTION DATABASE IS READ-ONLY FOR CLAUDE — every diagnostic above came from `SELECT`s, and the deploy is the owner's.** **DEPLOYED STATE 2026-09-09: backend `4ee2c752` (`v0.136.0`), frontend `7f371e65` (`v0.135.0`), migrations current at `V143`.** **Routing: CLAUDE CODE**; item 2 re-routed once its shape is decided. Full scope in `RELEASES.md`.

## What's next — sequenced 2026-09-06, after `v0.119.1` shipped

**⚠️ READ THIS BEFORE SCANNING THE BACKLOG INDEX FOR CANDIDATES.** Seven plans were written 2026-09-05;
four have shipped (`v0.117.0`, `v0.118.0`, `v0.119.0`, `v0.119.1`). This section records what remains and
**in what order**, so a fresh session does not re-derive it from the plans themselves.

### 1. ✅ SHIPPED AS `v0.120.0` (Released 2026-09-06) — Canonical note title integrity (`docs/claude-plans/canonical-note-title-generation-integrity-stage1.md`)

**⚠️ NO LONGER A CANDIDATE — IT SHIPPED.** With it done, **item 2 below is now the head of the queue** (and is blocked on an owner conversation, not on engineering). **⚠️ THE ALE REVIEW SET CURATOR PASS IS NOW FULLY UNBLOCKED:** its ordering constraint was this fix, so new ALE notes can be authored through Bulk Generate without acquiring contaminated titles. The three owner decisions below were taken at
kickoff on 2026-09-06; **(a) added an opt-in suggestion AGAINST the recommendation, and (c) was first
taken in and then DEFERRED on a finding** (there is no title-producing call to narrow — see the Current
Release Baseline and (c)'s own Backlog row). **Read the baseline, not this section, for what shipped.**
What follows is the candidate framing as written on 2026-09-06, kept because its ordering argument and
anti-drift still bind.

**The only remaining item that is a LIVE, REPRODUCED DEFECT ON CANONICAL CONTENT rather than a plan for
a capability.** A curator entered `Site Grading Principles` in Bulk Generate and the persisted title
became `Site Grading Principles in Civil Engineering` — with Civil Engineering **not** among the four
selected Applicable Programs.

**⚠️ THE CAUSE IS NEITHER OF THE TWO SUSPECTED ONES, AND RE-DERIVING THEM IS WASTED WORK.** It is not
context contamination and not a missing prompt rule; the audit closes both (§3, §4). It is one
unconditional mutation: **Bulk Generate creates the Note with the curator's topic as its title, then
overwrites it with the STUDY PACK's generated title** — `applyBulkGeneratedMetadataToNote` does
`note.setTitle(normalizeEditableTitle(generated.title()))`. Single-note generation never does this.
**The fix deletes a later mutation rather than adding logic.**

**⚠️ WHY IT IS ORDERED FIRST, AND IT IS AN ORDERING ARGUMENT RATHER THAN A SEVERITY ONE.** `v0.118.0`'s
locked contract is *"the Note's current title is the topic"*, so a contaminated title becomes the **seed
for regenerated content** — `…in Civil Engineering` would shape the body of every future regeneration of
that note. **Fix titles before regenerating the canonical library**, or the ALE pass has to be redone.

**⚠️ EXISTING NOTES ARE SAFE TODAY — verified in code during `v0.119.0`, not assumed.**
`applyBulkGeneratedMetadataToNote` is **unreachable from regeneration**: the bulk driver passes
`preservedSubject = null` deliberately, and single-note regeneration passes
`autoApplyGeneratedMetadata = false`. The exposure is **authoring NEW notes via Bulk Generate**.

**Three genuine owner decisions, each with the audit's recommendation** — take them at kickoff rather
than mid-implementation: (a) should the curator topic always win, or should an explicit *"suggest a
better title"* action exist? *(recommend: topic wins; no suggestion action — that is a feature, not part
of stopping the regression)*; (b) what happens to LLM tags once the title is not applied? *(recommend:
keep them, derived from the curator's topic rather than the discarded generated title)*; (c) is the
narrowed title-input contract wanted? *(recommend: defer — the context was already clean, so it would
not have prevented this)*.

**⚠️ ANTI-DRIFT FROM THE AUDIT: do NOT add Course/Program suffixes by default, and do NOT infer a title
from Applicable Programs, Review Set, Subject Plan or Domain Context.** No migration, no renames of
existing notes, no regeneration pass — the audit is explicit that Stage 1 covers none of those.

### 2. ✅ SHIPPED AS `v0.121.0` (Released 2026-09-06) — shared quiz recipient experience

**⚠️ NO LONGER A BLOCKED CANDIDATE — THE OWNER CONVERSATION HAPPENED AND THE RELEASE IS OPEN.** §1's three contradictions were read and resolved at kickoff, and the slice-4 provenance rule was owner-ruled the same day; **read the Current Release Baseline above, not this section, for what is being built.** `v0.121.0` takes **slices 1-4**; **slice 5 (Learning Connection integration) remains a separate follow-up release** and the supporter-progress audit travels with it. What follows is the candidate framing as written 2026-09-06, kept because its pairing rule and its "read §1 first" warning still bind.

**⚠️ SLICE 5 (`Learning Connection integration`) IS DEFERRED, NOT CANCELLED, AND IT IS GATED ON A DATE (2026-09-06).** It was the natural next release and was declined on evidence: a read-only production query returned **1 `linked_learner_relationships` row, 1 `ACCEPTED`, 2 invitations, 3 live grants**, so slice 5 would enrich a **supporter** surface for a population of one. **⚠️ TWO SEPARATE THINGS GATE IT AND THEY MUST NOT BE COLLAPSED. (a) EVIDENCE:** `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]`'s own instruction on a non-zero `ACCEPTED` count is ***"re-read at the next release rather than expand"*** — so slice 5 waits on that re-read. **(b) CONTAMINATION:** that same read is **13 days out** and its kill criterion keys on `ACCEPTED`, so **`v0.122.0` must not promote Learning Connections at all** — not the nav position, not a guidance tip. **That half unblocks after `2026-09-19` regardless of what the re-read concludes.** **⚠️ ONE PIECE OF SLICE 5 IS SHARPER THAN THE REST AND MUST NOT DRIFT INTO A "SMALL COPY RELEASE" LATER: §7's SENDER CONTEXT LOOKS LIKE COPY AND IS NOT.** It resolves a quiz owner's display name onto an **anonymous `permitAll` payload**, gated on a relationship predicate — an identity disclosure whose correctness IS a visibility predicate. **Whenever it is built it carries its own verification tier (ONE SCOPED COLD AGENT framed as falsification), and it is explicitly OUT of `v0.122.0`.**

#### Original candidate framing — shared quiz recipient experience + supporter progress

`docs/claude-plans/shared-quiz-recipient-experience-plan.md` and
`docs/claude-plans/supporter-progress-visibility-audit.md` are **companions and must be read together**;
the recipient plan's §8 supersedes the supporter audit where they differ.

**⚠️ DO NOT KICK EITHER OFF AS-IS.** The recipient plan opens by stating that **three owner decisions are
contradicted by the repo** — not disagreements with the direction, but *missing substrate the direction
assumes exists*. **Read its §1 before anything else.** That is an owner conversation first and a release
second.

### 3. EXPLICITLY NOT SCHEDULED — quiz assignments

`docs/claude-plans/quiz-assignments-learning-connections-stage1.md` is marked **NOT IMPLEMENTED, NOT
SCHEDULED** by its own author. It exists to stop the shared-quiz work drifting into the wrong
abstraction (*"Sharing is lightweight. Assignment is relational."*). **It is not a candidate; do not
scope it because it sits beside two plans that are.**

## Prioritization Lens & Strategic Frame (added 2026-07-28 — read before scoping or reprioritizing anything below)

> ### Ratified product vision — 2026-08-05. Read this first; it governs everything below.
>
> **NoteLib is a learning system built on top of a knowledge library.** The library is the foundation; **the learning journey is the product.**
>
> Three layers, earned strictly in order:
>
> 1. **Trust** — comprehensive Official Review Sets, so a learner can say *"I can trust NoteLib to cover my review."*
> 2. **Habit** — Study Packs, Companion, Progress, and Review Sets forming the system they return to.
> 3. **Community** — high-quality user-created and shared knowledge, once the first two are earned.
>
> **This confirms and sharpens the "journey, not the note" frame below rather than replacing it.** What it adds is the *ordering*, and one prioritization consequence: **community content is no longer the primary acquisition strategy.** Long-term, users creating and sharing their own notes is still absolutely the goal — the vision is not abandoned, it is being earned in the correct order. Only after a learner trusts NoteLib's coverage does the product earn the right to become their personal knowledge library.
>
> **What this means when scoping:** work that deepens Official Review Set comprehensiveness or the learning system outranks work that grows user-generated content as a funnel. Do not propose UGC as top-of-funnel acquisition; do not read the de-emphasis as the community feature being cut. Full statement in `docs/product/SPEC.md` → Product Philosophy.

This section exists so a fresh session doesn't have to re-derive from scratch what "NoteLib's center of gravity has shifted toward the learner's journey, not the note" actually means for prioritization. It was added after a strategic reprioritization discussion prompted by noticing that recent releases (Dashboard, Progress, Companion, Coach, Official Review Sets, Reusable Practice Assets) skew journey-centric rather than notes-first.

**This is not a new pivot — it's a long-held bet becoming visible, and internal strategy is already ahead of it.** "A Note Collection is a trackable readiness journey, not a folder" has been the stated direction since v0.33.0; "NoteLib as a personal learning OS" was ratified internally with GPT on 2026-07-23 (5 days before this note), as part of the Company Redefinition effort (see that section above). What changed isn't the direction — it's that enough journey-layer surface area now exists to see the shape in aggregate. Treat this as confirmation the existing direction is compounding, not a trigger to re-litigate it.

**The one real, un-actioned gap this surfaced: external marketing copy hasn't caught up to internal strategy.** The landing page still says "your notes become your study system" (notes-first); internal strategy has said learning-OS-centric for 5 days. See the Backlog Index row below — since superseded by the **Messaging Architecture** row. **Two parts of this note are now out of date:** the conversion-test condition was explicitly lifted for positioning copy by owner ratification 2026-08-01 (narrative consistency with the product vision is a design decision, not an optimization experiment; conversion is measured post-launch instead of gating rollout — pricing/checkout *mechanics* changes still need evidence), and "small, not urgent" undersold it — the ratified initiative is product-wide. The landing page itself is still un-actioned and is now tracked in that row's remaining-surfaces list.

**Prioritization filter — use to triage new candidates, weighted, not flat.** Does a candidate move: (1) acquisition, (2) return rate, (3) study effectiveness, (4) reusable practice assets, (5) the creator ecosystem, or (6) does it fix something broken / protect trust (bug fixes, data integrity, paywall correctness — always in scope regardless of the other five)? While retention (2.4% W1→W2, unmoved across three prior attempts) remains the binding constraint, (2) should dominate; (1) and (3)-(5) are tie-breakers, not co-equal boxes to check. Don't let "this touches box N a little" alone justify a build — that's how feature creep re-enters.

**Guardrail: don't let Challenge Quiz's recent momentum keep pulling the next discretionary pick.** The heavy Challenge Quiz investment this cycle (v0.60.1/v0.60.2/v0.60.3/v0.61.0) was mostly bug-driven, not strategy-driven — v0.60.0's template sharing surfaced a real production bug cluster that had to get fixed, and familiarity kept pulling more work into that same corner afterward. That work was necessary and is now done; Challenge Quiz is stable. The next discretionary pick after v0.61.0 should default toward between-session/Coach/Progress work (the actual proven bottleneck — see the "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc) unless a new production bug forces otherwise.

**Loops, not just features — see the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc**, which now names three loops (Acquisition, Learning, Creator) and is honest about which ones are actually running today versus asserted or unproven. Use it as a diagnostic — naming something a loop doesn't make it spin.

## Backlog Index (read this before deciding what's next — do not scan prose to find candidates)

**Six rows moved to `docs/archive/ROADMAP_ARCHIVE.md` on 2026-09-11** (item 6 of
`docs/claude-plans/context-doc-token-reduction-plan.md`, run for the first time — deliberately held
back at `v0.126.0` pending per-row judgment). Each carried an unambiguous shipped/resolved status
with no live checkpoint and no remaining action; the criterion and full text are at the archive
entry. This is a MOVE, not a delete, and no row carrying a live due-dated checkpoint gate or an
indexed planning artifact was touched — verified by exact checkpoint-count and file-coverage
parity before and after.

**Invariant: no PLANNING OR CURATION ARTIFACT may exist anywhere in `docs/` without a row here** — `docs/claude-prompt/*-out/` directories, `docs/claude-plans/` files, `docs/claude-findings/` reports, and `docs/curriculum/` workbooks and queries alike. **⚠️ THE RULE IS STATED BY PROPERTY NOW, BECAUSE NAMING DIRECTORIES HAS BEEN OUTGROWN TWICE.** It first named only `*-out/`, and the `v0.71.1` kickoff found five unindexed `docs/claude-plans/` files; it was widened to name that directory too, and the `v0.97.0` signoff then found six unindexed files in a **new** `docs/curriculum/`, which the rule did not mention. A list of directories is always one new directory behind. **The test is what the file IS — does anything remain to be done with it — not where it lives.** A Fable session that writes planning output adds its row in the same commit. This rule exists because Smart Review Planning (7 fully-architected documents in `docs/claude-prompt/fable-out/`) sat undiscovered for ~5 release cycles, found only by chance when the user asked about it directly — a prose mention buried in a released-version paragraph is not an index entry.

**One narrow exemption, stated 2026-08-10 at the `v0.71.1` kickoff rather than left to be re-derived: a *release artifact* is covered by its version's own row and does not need one of its own.** A release artifact is a file written to serve one release's decision and then finished — a consultation prompt sent to GPT, a sizing query run once against production, an audit result. It is not planning output waiting to be scheduled, which is what this invariant exists to catch. The distinction is *does anything remain to be done with it*, not which directory it lives in. Currently exempt, each traceable through its release's section above: `b2-migration-artifact-sizing.sql` and `c1-multi-program-null-domain-sizing.sql` (`v0.71.0`), **⚠️ plus two that were exempt and are now DELETED in the `v0.97.0` docs cleanup, 2026-08-29** — `b2-learner-owned-applicable-programs-consultation-prompt.md` and `onboarding-intent-router-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md`, both `v0.71.0`, both answered and shipped (B2 became `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*; the Intent Router became `v0.71.0` slice 5, whose live row cites its **plan** file and audit SQL, not the prompt). **⚠️ THIS SENTENCE IS A LIVE REGISTRY, NOT A DATED LOG, which is why it is edited rather than left standing** — the historical-log-entry ruling (leave dated scan notes intact) does not extend to a list that asserts what is exempt *now*; leaving it would have claimed exemption for files that no longer exist. Also `item1-admin-authored-applicable-programs-sizing.sql` (`v0.71.1`, run 2026-08-11, results recorded in `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*), plus `v0.67.0-explore-owns-discovery-ia.md` (`v0.67.0`, shipped in full). **Added 2026-09-05 at the `v0.119.0` kickoff scan, after a naive basename grep flagged all four:** `authoring-and-quiz-legibility-gpt-tightening-prompt.md` (`v0.117.0`, a GPT tightening prompt, answered and shipped) and `v0.115.0-stage1-adoption-update-audit.md`, `v0.115.0-stage1-domain-context-audit.md`, `v0.115.0-stage1-note-copy-audit.md` (`v0.115.0`, audit results for a Released version whose live row cites `v0.115.0-stage1-master-architecture-reconciliation.md`). **If a `.sql` or consultation prompt ever carries an unrun query or an unanswered question past its release's signoff, it stops being an artifact and needs a row.**

**Cite rows by TITLE, never by line number.** Added 2026-08-14 after the `v0.75.0` pressure test found three citations pointing at the wrong rows: `RELEASES.md` and this file cited "row 185" and "row 187", both correct when written, both wrong once the kickoff commit inserted two lines above the index. **This recurs by construction** — every kickoff edits text above this table — and one of the broken pointers was the stated evidence for a design decision, so the reader would have landed on an unrelated row and found no support for it.

**⚠️ COUNTING THIS TABLE MECHANICALLY IS UNRELIABLE, AND THE FAILURE IS REPEATABLE — added 2026-09-03 after deriving THREE different counts of the same September cluster in one session.** Two traps, both hit: **(a) ROWS ARE NOT A FIXED WIDTH.** Most have 5 content cells, but several carry `|` inside their prose — the Retention H1+H5 row has **7** — so `awk '{print $(NF-1)}'` and `cells[-2]` read **Last reviewed**, not **Gate**. That mis-read produced a confident claim that `2026-09-10` *"is not a gate date at all"*, which is **FALSE**: it is `v0.72.0`'s live proximal retention checkpoint. **A correction based on it was written and discarded before it reached the record only because the row was opened and read.** **(b) A MENTION IS NOT AN OBLIGATION.** Rows cross-reference each other's checkpoints — *"onboarding stays frozen until `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]`"* is a constraint citing a date, not a read owed by that row — so grepping dates over-counts, while grepping only titles under-counts the ones declared in the Gate cell. **⚠️ THE RELIABLE METHOD IS TO OPEN THE ROW.** Establish per row whether it OWNS a dated read or merely cites one; do not report a count derived by pattern alone, and do not "correct" a previous count without opening the rows behind both. **A wrong correction is worse than the wrong number, because it arrives with the authority of a fix.**

**⚠️ CHECKPOINT GATE ANSWERED AT THE `v0.118.0` SIGNOFF, 2026-09-05 — NONE OWED, AND THE REASONING IS RECORDED RATHER THAN THE VERDICT ALONE.** The gate asks whether anything shipped ahead of its own evidence. The regeneration feature did not: it was built against an owner-ratified plan and verified by the declared falsification pass, and it is a capability rather than a bet on a metric. **The one genuine candidate is the owner override that removed the AI metadata suggestion from plain Study Pack regeneration** (2026-09-05) — an owner override is a listed trigger. **It cannot carry a VALID checkpoint, and that is why none was written: there is no instrumentation for the suggestion at all** — `AnalyticsEventType` contains no suggestion event and none fires anywhere — **and the gate states outright that a checkpoint without a working metric is decorative.** Shipping an event at signoff to manufacture one would be exactly the drift this release's own anti-drift forbids (*no analytics-event change*). **So it is recorded as a Backlog row with a re-read trigger instead of a dated read**, which is the honest form. **⚠️ Do NOT re-derive this as a missing checkpoint at the next kickoff — step 9 cannot distinguish "never owed" from "never written", which is why it is written down here.**

**⚠️ THE CLUSTER FIGURE CARRIED BY THE LAST FOUR SCAN NOTES WAS WRONG, AND IT WAS WRONG BY CONSTRUCTION.** Every scan from `v0.103.0` through `v0.106.0` recorded *"27 rows on `2026-09-11`"*. **That is a MENTION count, not a row count** — a `grep -c` over a file whose Backlog Index intro is itself thick with prose cross-references to those dates, so the scan notes were counting their own predecessors. Re-derived here by parsing the table body row by row (159 table lines; **142 live, 9 struck**) and reading each row's own title and `Gate` cell: **32 LIVE rows touch `2026-09-10`–`2026-09-17`**, and the useful split is not the total but the KIND — **~12 are CHECKPOINT READS** (production SQL with kill criteria already written: `v0.72.0` retention H1+H5 on `2026-09-10`; `v0.74.0` ×2 on `2026-09-12`; `v0.79.0`, `v0.78.0`, `v0.80.0` on `2026-09-14`; `v0.81.0` on `2026-09-15`; `v0.82.0` ×2, `v0.83.0`, `v0.84.0` on `2026-09-16`; `v0.85.0` on `2026-09-17`) and **~20 are PARKED WORK ITEMS** the window unblocks (the whole onboarding set, Target Audience Phases 2–4, Discovery System, the LaTeX rendering gap). **⚠️ THE DISTINCTION IS THE POINT: the cluster is a READING workload, not a building one**, which is what made it safe to scope a feature release across it rather than reserving a slot. **Recorded at length because a wrong number was carried forward four times unchallenged, and it was the stated input to a sequencing decision each time** — the exact status-staleness failure this intro keeps warning about, appearing inside step 9's own bookkeeping for the second time.

**Gate types, added 2026-07-28 (pressure-tested via advisor + an independent Opus review — see the "Prioritization Lens" section above for why this was needed).** Every `Gate` cell below should read as one of four types, tagged inline. The problem this fixes: an EVIDENCE gate and a DECISION gate both render as "blocked," but only one is actually waiting on data — a DECISION gate is unblockable by more querying, it just needs the owner to choose. Conflating them makes the whole roadmap feel uniformly data-blocked when a real chunk of it is one decision away.

- **`[EVIDENCE]`** — genuinely needs production data or a real-world signal before building. The strict-gating discipline applies in full.
- **`[DECISION]`** — waiting on an explicit owner call, not on more data. Actionable today by having the conversation, not by running a query.
- **`[EFFORT]`** — needs a scoping pass, curator capacity, or non-engineering execution (outreach, a design pass). Not data-blocked, resource- or attention-blocked.
- **`[CHECKPOINT — due YYYY-MM-DD]`** — an item that shipped ahead of evidence (an `EVIDENCE` gate was invalid — see the bootstrap test below) and carries a dated, falsifiable post-build obligation instead of a pre-build gate. Must have all five: a date, a named kill criterion, live in this column (not buried in Status prose), scanned by the review ritual above, and its measurement instrumentation shipped in the same release — a checkpoint without a working metric is decorative (see `08-diagnostic-read-methodology.md`'s v0.48.0 digest-lift postmortem for what an unmeasurable checkpoint looks like in practice).

**The bootstrap test — when an `EVIDENCE` gate is actually invalid and the item should ship as `DECISION` + `CHECKPOINT` instead.** All three must hold, not just one:
1. The feature itself is the mechanism that would produce the gate's own metric (not merely correlated with it) — e.g. Knowledge Impact is the only lever that rewards publishing, so waiting for publish-rate evidence before building the thing that would move publish rate is circular. (Contrast: Bulk Quiz's teacher-count gate is *not* invalid this way — teachers sign up for reasons unrelated to Bulk Quiz, so "we can't know until it exists" doesn't apply.)
2. It fits one release slot and is reversible — no schema/pricing/nav commitment that needs a migration to withdraw. This clause is what stops the bootstrap argument from justifying unlimited spend.
3. The checkpoint metric will actually be operable at current scale in the stated window — not still a single-digit denominator when the date arrives. This is the clause that would have caught Knowledge Impact's original re-check design (re-running the same 3-creator-count query) before it shipped; the fix is a conditional-rate event (did a creator who viewed their impact dashboard publish again within N days), not a raw count.

**⚠️ CHECKPOINT CONSOLIDATION — 2026-08-16. Read this before acting on any dated row below.** The table had accumulated **~24 dated obligations across 18 rows, spread over nine separate September dates.** That is not a discipline, it is overhead: nine reading sessions against one analytics table, in one window, at a scale where most of the rates involved have denominators in the tens. **This applies clause 3 of the bootstrap test retroactively** — it was always the rule (*"operable at current scale … not still a single-digit denominator when the date arrives"*), it was simply never enforced against checkpoints already written.

**Nine dates collapse to two. The per-row metrics, kill criteria and caveats below all still stand — only the date changes.**

- **`2026-09-11` — the onboarding funnel read, alone.** It keeps its own date because it has a real denominator (375 signups against a 62.4% completion baseline), it gates concrete work (the onboarding catalog-first follow-up), and reading it early is worthless. Rows: onboarding redesign, catalog-first-in-onboarding.
- **`2026-09-16` — everything else, as ONE batch.** Queries are already written in `docs/claude-plans/september-2026-checkpoint-reads.sql`. **Query 0 (delivery health) is read first and conditions all the rest**, which is itself an argument for batching: run separately, eight of these reads are interpreted before the number that tells you whether their events even arrived. Rows: `v0.78.0`, `v0.79.0` (proximal), `v0.80.0`, `v0.81.0`, `v0.82.0`, `v0.74.0` ×3, post-mastery next step, Adaptive Practice entry attribution.
- **Unchanged:** distal reads (`2026-10-15`, `2026-11-09`), far-future architectural dates (`2027-02-01`), and the `2026-09-30` Challenge Quiz adoption reads — different windows, not the same batch.

**What earns its own date from here on.** Being wrong is expensive and hard to reverse (a migration that writes data, anything that destroys evidence — `v0.82.0`'s is the model), **or** the denominator is genuinely large (funnel rates over all signups, total event volume). **What does not:** a conversion rate on a surface only a handful of learners reach. Write those down, batch them, and accept that *"too small to read"* is the likely finding — it is still a finding, it just does not deserve a calendar entry of its own.

**⚠️ Two gates were converted to recorded confounds rather than kept, 2026-08-16 (owner call).** The Target Audience retirement and the academic-levels program cleanup were both gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-13]` because they change Public Library discovery. **They now ship without waiting**, with the confound written down instead — the same call `v0.78.0` made and recorded. The reasoning: since `v0.79.0` the course/program chips are the primary filter vocabulary and audience is secondary, so removing it is a marginal confound rather than a fatal one. **This does NOT extend to dropping `notes.target_profile_type`**, which stays until `2026-09-16` reports — it is the input `V117` derived depth from, and dropping it would make that checkpoint's own kill criterion unexecutable.

holds are duration-bound, not throughput-bound, so a larger pool buys time proportional to nothing — **and raising it without lowering `threads.max` changes which limit binds first.** **⚠️ THE HEALTH-CHECK-ON-THE-SAME-POOL LEG IS STILL OPEN FROM 2026-09-04 and is the ONE change that would have prevented the RESTART — though not the errors — in all three incidents.** **Two further items owe their own rows and did NOT cause this: §5's still-unbounded anonymous reads (`NoteCollectionService.listPublic`, `/course-programs`, `/subjects`, `/tags`) — calibrated as latent, since 5 public root collections cannot exhaust a 20-connection pool — and §8's ramp-window exposure, where an instance passes its health check and serves traffic holding only 1–7 connections.** | 2026-09-10 |

| **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-07]` — has the user base grown to where a single announcement publish can starve the connection pool?** | `v0.130.0` signoff, 2026-09-07 (full three-agent pressure test, backend agent finding D1) | **THE FAN-OUT SHIPPED SYNCHRONOUSLY INSIDE THE ADMIN PUBLISH REQUEST, AND IT HOLDS ONE OF TWENTY HIKARI CONNECTIONS FOR ITS ENTIRE DURATION.** With `open-in-view=ON` and `DELAYED_ACQUISITION_AND_HOLD` the connection is held until the HTTP request ends, not until each insert commits, so a publish to N recipients is N transactions and ~2N prepared statements on **one held connection**, with the persistence context never cleared (O(N²) growth). **⚠️ THE BLAST RADIUS IS THE WHOLE APPLICATION, NOT THE ADMIN'S REQUEST** — a large publish can starve the pool every learner request draws from. **⚠️ This is the exact shape of BOTH recorded production outages**, which is why the release's original wording (*"a scaling ceiling rather than a correctness gap"*) was replaced. **⚠️ THE INSTRUMENTATION IS ALREADY SHIPPED AND NEEDS NOTHING NEW, WHICH IS WHY THIS IS NOT DECORATIVE:** the publish response already returns `recipientCount` / `delivered` / `skipped`, and the audience size is a plain read — `SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE status = 'ACTIVE'`, plus the same count per `profile_type`. **⚠️ THE DENOMINATOR IS EVERY ACTIVE ACCOUNT, so unlike most rows in this table it is large by construction and cannot come back "too small to read".** | **KILL CRITERION, STATED BEFORE THE READ: if the largest resolvable audience exceeds 500 active users, synchronous fan-out stops being acceptable and moving it off the request thread becomes the next release's work — not a later optimisation.** Between 200 and 500, admins must be told to publish off-peak and the row is re-dated. Below 200 it holds as-is. **⚠️ THE REAL TRIGGER IS SCALE, NOT THE CALENDAR — the date exists only so something scans it.** If the user base crosses 500 before 2026-10-07, the checkpoint is due then, not on its date. **⚠️ Do NOT discharge this by raising the connection pool** (`AppConfig:52-72`, a `v0.112.0` Phase 3 decision gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-04]`) — that trades one starvation mode for another and is explicitly forbidden by the performance-audit row below. **⚠️ A PASS IS NOT PROOF THE DESIGN IS SOUND, only that it has not yet been exercised:** no announcement has been published to a large audience, so a quiet read may mean the feature is unused rather than safe — check `SELECT count(*) FROM announcements WHERE status <> 'DRAFT'` before reading a pass as reassurance. | 2026-09-07 |

| **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-05]` — do learners APPLY offered updates, and does the apply path put the connection pool back under load?** | `v0.116.0` signoff | **⚠️ TWO QUESTIONS ON ONE DATE, AND THE SECOND IS THE SHARPER ONE.** **(a) UPTAKE — shipped on a DEMAND INFERENCE, not a measurement:** 10 no-op re-adopt attempts by 4 distinct users were read as learners asking for updates. Suggestive, not uptake — those presses may equally have been attempts to RESET a plan, or mis-clicks on a button that appeared to do nothing. **(b) LOAD — an apply holds ONE pooled connection for the entire request** (`DELAYED_ACQUISITION_AND_HOLD`, open-in-view ON, read from `ConnectionLifetimeStartupLogger` rather than framework defaults), up to **1,204 `copyNote` calls** if every learner applies, against `maximum-pool-size: 20` and a 5 s acquisition timeout — **and `inspectSourceUpdate` is N+1 by plan and runs on EVERY page load of all 523 adopted collections.** This is a new long-holding request path added **one release after the 2026-09-04 pool-exhaustion outage.** Query written at signoff: `docs/claude-plans/v0.116.0-additive-updates-checkpoint-read.sql` (Q1 uptake, **Q2 the offer side that makes Q1 interpretable**, Q3 the restructure population, Q4 item-4 reachability). | **KILL CRITERIA, STATED BEFORE THE READ. (b) If 5xx or acquisition-timeout rates rise materially, the apply path must move off a single request-scoped connection BEFORE further work lands on it** — that is the outage mechanism, not a slow page. **⚠️ Q2 IS WHAT MAKES Q1 INTERPRETABLE and must not be skipped:** a low apply rate against a low offer rate means nothing was ever offered, which is NOT learner disinterest. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE: 92 learners hold adoptions and only 28 have pending additions. "Too few to read" is a RE-DATE for (a) — and itself a finding, since it would mean the stranded population is smaller than 364 stranded placements implied. It is NEVER a re-date for (b): a load problem does not need a large denominator to be real.** **⚠️ Q3 IS A REGRESSION TRIPWIRE, NOT A METRIC:** an adoption holding direct notes whose source has children must report MOVED and apply nothing — if any gained children, the fix regressed and that is a P0. | 2026-09-05 |

the IA move is a discoverability fix, not a performance one.** **⚠️ SECOND BRIEF IN A ROW WHOSE CENTRAL REFACTOR PREMISE WAS FALSE** (`v0.133.0` asked to generalize an already-generic shortcut) — both failed the same way, by describing coupling the code does not have. **✅ Premise 1 IS true and now measured: `KNOWLEDGE_IMPACT_DASHBOARD_VIEWED` has fired SEVEN TIMES in the product's lifetime.** **DENOMINATOR IS ONE — and the usual deferral does NOT transfer:** the single account with impact is the **owner's own ADMIN account** (1,587 public notes, 56 learners helped), the owner is the one asking, and 56 is *measured* impact rather than a speculative event stream. **⚠️ `PUBLIC_NOTE_VIEWED` has 25,262 rows and ZERO with a `user_id`** — not a bug (`PUBLIC_NOTE_COPIED` has 5,586/5,586), just all-anonymous traffic — **so views can NEVER be deduplicated per person and the UI must say "page views"**. **SIZE/SEQUENCING: 7 required items, past the 3–4 band, so the audit recommends TWO releases — R1 = bound the query + add a summary endpoint + switch Settings (the actual defect, cheapest verification); Your Impact = how my shared knowledge is helping other learners. | **Curator bulk regeneration — Stage 1 architecture & UX audit** | `docs/claude-plans/curator-bulk-regeneration-stage1.md`, 2026-09-05; frontend/product by the session, backend bulk infrastructure by a cold agent reading real code, every claim `file:line`-anchored | **⚠️ INDEXED AT THE `v0.118.0` KICKOFF — the THIRD unindexed plan file in three releases; step 8 has now caught one every cycle.** **STAGE 1 ONLY. §29 verdict: bulk regeneration is justified and safe to BUILD, and explicitly NOT safe to ship in the same release as the single-Note primitive** — which is why `v0.118.0` is Phase 1 alone. **⚠️ TWO FINDINGS DECIDE ITS SHAPE, BOTH ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN OPINION: (1) the existing bulk receipt CANNOT report a batch this long** — the client poller gives up after ~5 minutes and tolerates only ~4 s of 404s, while the receipt row is written only in `processBatch`'s `finally`, and regeneration is strictly slower per item (TWO LLM calls, not one), so **reusing the receipt as-is guarantees the curator never learns a real batch's outcome**; **(2) the batch loop and each item's Study Pack work share ONE 2-thread executor**, and the loop holds one of those threads for the batch's whole duration. | **Blocked on `v0.118.0` shipping first.** Both findings must be solved before bulk — the receipt needs durable semantics rather than ephemeral, and the executor contention needs resolving. **⚠️ Do NOT inherit the existing receipt component; §29 says it must be REPLACED.** | 2026-09-05 |
| **`[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-05]` — did clearing TRANSFER classification authority, or just remove representation?** | `v0.115.0` signoff | **SHIPPED ON A BOOTSTRAP ARGUMENT FROM MECHANISM, AND THAT IS WHY IT OWES A READ.** The release's central claim is that clearing the copied rows **un-shadows** the learner's own Course / Program field, which the editor then renders with a required marker (`note-editor-form.tsx:427-433`) — so authority is **transferred**, not merely revoked. **That the learner then actually classifies is INFERRED FROM THE MECHANISM, never measured.** If they publish and never fill it in, the release traded *shelved under the curator's programs* for **not shelved at all** — a discovery regression wearing the shape of a correctness fix. **⚠️ SHARES ITS DATE with the row above by the consolidation rule; the subjects are unrelated and the reads run in one sitting.** **Instrumentation needs nothing new and was VERIFIED FIRING at signoff:** `PUBLIC_NOTE_PUBLISHED` (`NoteService:505`) fires on the **identical** `becomingPublic` predicate as the clearing, so the post-deploy cohort is exactly identifiable, and the outcome reads straight off `notes` + `note_course_program`. Query written at signoff: `docs/claude-plans/v0.115.0-publication-authority-checkpoint-read.sql` (Q1 headline, Q2 time-to-classify, **Q3 the control**, Q4 catalog blast-radius). | **KILL CRITERION, STATED BEFORE THE READ: if a MAJORITY of post-deploy learner publications remain UNSHELVED (zero join rows AND null `course_program`), the un-shadowing did not transfer authority — it removed representation. Remedy is a PUBLISH-TIME classification step, NOT reverting the clearing**, which would restore the curator-authority defect the release closed. **⚠️ Q3 IS WHAT MAKES Q1 INTERPRETABLE and must not be skipped:** learner-authored public notes were never shadowed, so their classification rate is the baseline — a low Q1 matched by an equally low Q3 means the finding is about **the field**, not this release, and must not be attributed to the clearing. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE, BINDING: ZERO learner copies were public on 2026-09-05, so this cohort starts EMPTY.** Too small to read is **itself the finding** — it would mean learners essentially never publish copies, making the closed hazard more theoretical than 536 rows suggested. **Record that and CLOSE; do not silently re-date.** | 2026-09-05 |

Note copy: **both options D and C are REFUTED** (C fires on zero notes; D is refuted by 608 null-string / 551 multi-row source notes) — the fix is one write path at the publish transition. They are INDEPENDENT of Slices 1-2 and do NOT hold early-adopter work behind taxonomy expansion.** | 2026-09-05 |

The instrument reached production at `2026-09-04 10:33:49 UTC`** (v0.112.0 deploy; v0.113.1 current from 15:35:08 UTC), so the 30 days run from there and `2026-10-04` is correctly dated rather than approximate. **An interim read over 10:33 → 15:50 UTC returns ZERO traces — and that CLEARS NOTHING:** 5.3 hours of a quiet afternoon is what **both** surviving hypotheses predict, and the incident itself was preceded by five minutes of total silence. **⚠️ MORE IMPORTANTLY, §12 OF THE FINDING SHIFTS THE PRIOR BETWEEN (a) AND (b) BEFORE THIS READ EVEN RUNS:** the §5 queries were finally run and the write side is **empty on a widened window** — zero sessions 05:00–05:56, zero notes enqueued 04:00–05:56 — against a root-cause class needing ~7 concurrent generations. **So (a) is now positively unsupported, which means criterion (i) is the LEAST likely outcome and criterion (ii) — Phase 3 gets RESCOPED — is the one to be ready for.** **⚠️ Do NOT let that shift become a reason to answer this checkpoint by argument; it is the reason not to.** | 2026-09-04 |
| **2026-09-04 production outage — HikariCP pool exhaustion → health-check failure → platform restart** | `docs/claude-findings/2026-09-04-prod-outage-hikari-pool-exhaustion.md` (diagnosis, 2026-09-04) + `docs/20260904_prod-issue-down.log` (763 lines, 05:55:07–05:57:00 UTC) | **`[SCOPED — v0.112.0]`.** Production was **killed for failing a health check it could no longer answer** — the pool was exhausted (`total=10, active=10, idle=0, waiting=15`) and `DataSourceHealthIndicator` starved on the same pool, failing at 30,002 ms; Render replaced the instance ~65 s later. **No crash, no OOM, and nothing recovered on its own.** **⚠️ EVERY LOAD-BEARING CLAIM RE-VERIFIED IN CODE AT THE `v0.112.0` KICKOFF:** there is **no HikariCP configuration anywhere**, so `total=10` is Hikari's **default** and **nobody chose 10**; `server.tomcat.threads.max: 25` makes the ratio **25 threads against 10 connections**; `ChallengeQuizService` is class-level `@Transactional` (`:84`) with the LLM **inside** (`:449`) at a **180 s** read timeout against a **30 s** acquisition timeout; `open-in-view` is **unset**, defaulting to `true`. **Fourteen paths hold a connection across an OpenAI call, so seven concurrent generations exhaust the pool.** **⚠️ THE FILE CARRIES ONE RETRACTED FINDING (§6, OCR — Vision is DISABLED in production, so the code-level observation stands and the production conclusion does not) AND ONE OPEN HYPOTHESIS (§7, OSIV) — do NOT cite §6 as a defect or §7 as a conclusion.** **⚠️ CORRECTED AT THE `v0.112.0` SIGNOFF — §3 IS NOT CONFIRMED AND IS WEAKER THAN THIS ROW ORIGINALLY IMPLIED.** The Render read found the pool fully checked out **while the database was IDLE** (CPU ~1–2% of a core, memory 140–171 MB of 256 MB) under **0–9 requests/min**, with **no app log output for the five minutes before the first symptom** and **scheduled jobs reporting nothing stuck**. That **refutes** a database-side stall and an anonymous read burst, and leaves **(a)** holds across slow external calls **or (b)** an unconsidered **connection LEAK** — which Phase 3 does nothing for. **See §11 of the finding; `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-10-04]` decides between them.** **⚠️ AMENDED 2026-09-04 (`v0.114.0`) — §5 WAS FINALLY RUN AND §11's REFUTATION OF THE READ BURST IS WITHDRAWN. See §12.** §11 called those queries *"largely moot"* and they were left unrun; they were not moot, they were unread. **Q1/Q3/Q4/Q5 are all zero — and Q7 is zero on all seven baseline days, so Q1's zero is that hour's NORMAL state and proves nothing alone.** Widening the window does: **zero sessions 05:00–05:56, zero notes enqueued 04:00–05:56**, so **(a) has ZERO supporting rows.** **⚠️ Q2 — the only query here that can see READS, and the one §11 never consulted — carries the finding, but ONLY at raw-timestamp resolution: the minute bucket misleads and a first pass misread it as broad traffic.** It is **ONE note hit SIXTEEN times in 78 seconds** (twelve inside eight seconds), all anonymous, all `pathType: seo`, on a page otherwise viewed **1–2 times an HOUR across eleven days** — a pattern occurring **once in that span**, during the outage, **beginning ~15 s BEFORE the saturation signature.** **§11 refuted the read burst from `http_request_count` alone — a metric §11 ITSELF recorded as possibly binning by COMPLETION rather than arrival, on a service whose request logs are not retained.** **⚠️ THE HYPOTHESIS IS LIVE AGAIN, NOT CONFIRMED, AND ITS DIRECTION IS UNSETTLED** — a stalled client reloading produces a similar shape and the trailing intervals read as backoff, though the event fires client-side **after render**, so each row is a load that completed. **⚠️ And Q2 is a FLOOR, never a total: ISR revalidation and crawlers run no client-side JavaScript and leave no row at all.** It is nonetheless the only surviving explanation with any positive evidence, while **Phase 3 is aimed at the one the data now argues against.** | **Un-parked and being fixed as `v0.112.0 — Connection Pool Integrity`.** This row closes when Phase 3 ships. **⚠️ It does NOT close on Phase 1**, which is mitigation: the pool stops being 10, but fourteen paths still hold a connection across an LLM call. | 2026-09-04 |

| **~~The recovery sweep never writes `generation_status_at`~~ — ALREADY FIXED, row was stale.** Closed 2026-08-26 | Found 2026-08-19 by read (d) of `docs/claude-plans/v0.86.0-checkpoint-read.sql`, then confirmed in code | **RESOLVED — and it was resolved before this row was last reviewed, which is the point worth recording.** `v0.89.0` shipped the fix (`CLAUDE.md` records it in that release's scope); `GenerationRecoveryRowWriter.recoverPool` now stamps `setGenerationStatusAt(OffsetDateTime.now(ZoneOffset.UTC))` beside the `FAILED` write. Verified in code 2026-08-26 while scoping further `v0.90.0` folds — **the row had survived two Backlog Index reviews describing work that no longer existed.** Original finding, retained for the record: `recoverPool:58-59` set `POOL_STATUS_FAILED` and saved, and **never called `setGenerationStatusAt(...)`** — it is a fifth status-write site beside the four lifecycle sites that do stamp. The read that exposed it: all 37 swept rows carry the identical microsecond `2026-08-18 05:08:09.415724`, which is the `V118` seed `UPDATE`, not the sweep. **Operationally harmless today** — a FAILED row is terminal, so a stale stamp cannot cause a re-sweep. **Two real costs:** (1) `generation_status_at` is supposed to mean *when the status last changed*, and on every swept row it means *when `V118` ran*; (2) **it makes the bounds half of `v0.86.0`'s checkpoint unanswerable** — read (b) exists to detect bounds that are too tight, which shows up as pools repeatedly swept and rebuilt, and with no sweep timestamp there is no way to tell a pool swept in August from one swept five minutes ago. `recoverLongExamSession` reads `getCreatedAt()` and is fine, because `buildGeneratingSession` always builds a new row. | **`[EFFORT]`, and it is small** — one `setGenerationStatusAt` call plus a test asserting the persisted stamp advances on sweep. **⚠️ Do this before re-reading the bounds question**, or that read produces a confident wrong answer rather than an honest unknown. | 2026-08-19 |

They are config-owned, so tightening is a config change, not a code change. | **Read 14 days after DEPLOY, not merge.** **KILL CRITERION, stated before the read:** if part (a) returns a non-zero count on any surface — any row still non-terminal past its own bound — the sweeper is **not working**, and the response is to diagnose it, **not** to tighten the bounds. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR CLAUSE:** if the backlog drained on the first post-deploy sweep and no deploy has interrupted generation since, then zero is consistent with *nothing to sweep* and is **not** evidence the sweeper works — that outcome is a **re-date, not a verdict**. Part (c) measures that directly. **Instrumentation:** no analytics event is involved and none is needed — the read keys on the two clock columns `V118` added, written by five verified call sites (four pool status writes, one note enqueue), so the metric is the table itself rather than an event that might not fire. **⚠️ EARLY READ RUN 2026-08-19 — the historical backlog DRAINED, which is NOT the same as the kill criterion passing, and the denominator clause above is what makes the difference.** Step 6a returned `FAILED/BOARD_EXAM 40` and `FAILED/LONG_EXAM 36` and **nothing else** — the query lists every non-`READY` status, so `GENERATING` = **0** and `PENDING` = **0**, down from the 37 that were stuck. That transition could not have come from normal use: recovery at `sampleQuestions:94` refuses to act while a row still reads `PENDING`/`GENERATING`, which was the defect. The 76 `FAILED` pools now self-heal through `refreshPool` on next use, which is the designed end state. **⚠️ But this is precisely the case the denominator clause anticipates:** it shows the sweeper fired at least once against a known backlog; it does **not** show it will catch a future stranding, because no deploy has interrupted generation since. **Part (c) still decides the verdict on 2026-09-01 — do not close this row on the early read.** **⚠️ Step 6a CANNOT attribute the change to the sweeper, and must not be quoted as if it does.** It selects `min/max(created_at)`, the clock `v0.86.0` itself declared unusable for pools because rows are reused — which is precisely why `V118` added `generation_status_at`. The `2026-07-03` timestamps therefore mean only *no new pool row has been inserted since then*, and a later reader could easily misread them as evidence the sweeper never fired. **Attribution query owed, one line:** `select generation_status, count(*), count(*) filter (where generation_status_at is null) as unstamped, min(generation_status_at), max(generation_status_at) from exam_question_pool where generation_status <> 'READY' group by 1;` — roughly 37 rows stamped at or after the `v0.86.0` deploy confirms the sweeper; all-`NULL` or all-pre-deploy means something else cleared them and the sweeper is still inert. **⚠️ Step 6b is still UNRUN, and it covers the more severe half** — the 1 Long Exam session stuck since 2026-05-19 was a *hard permanent block* (`LongExamService:169` hands the learner back the stuck session rather than creating one), where the pool damage only degraded exam starts to on-demand generation. **✅ VERDICT 2026-08-19 — PASS on the primary question, closed 18 days early.** Read (a) returned **0/0/0/0**: nothing past its bound on any of the four surfaces. Read (d) is what makes this a verdict rather than an absence: **76 FAILED pools, 39 unstamped, 37 stamped** — and **37 is exactly the stuck population** (18 `GENERATING` + 19 `PENDING`). `V118` seeded `generation_status_at` only for non-terminal rows, so those 37 *are* the previously-stuck set, and they are now all FAILED. Nothing but the sweeper transitions non-terminal → FAILED by age; recovery at `sampleQuestions:94` refuses to act on a non-terminal row, which was the whole defect. **This is what resolves the denominator clause** — there WAS a backlog, it was 37 rows, and it drained. Read (e) confirms the severe half: **the `LONG_EXAM` session stranded since 2026-05-19 is now `FAILED`**, so the hard permanent block is cleared. (The `IN_PROGRESS`/`LONG_EXAM` row from 2026-05-15 is an abandoned session, not a stuck one — `IN_PROGRESS` is deliberately not swept.) **⚠️ Residual, stated honestly: this proves the sweeper drained a KNOWN backlog once. It does not prove it catches a future stranding**, because no deploy has interrupted generation since. That residual is much weaker than the original question and does not justify holding the row open. **⚠️ The bounds half of this checkpoint is NOT answered and cannot be, until the stamp defect below is fixed** — see the `generation_status_at` row. | 2026-08-19 |
