Companion consultation prompt: `docs/claude-plans/linked-learners-surfacing-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md` | **Phase 1 shipped as `v0.91.0` — Shared Learning Material.** ⚠️ **The audit's headline finding stands independently of the plan: sharing a note meant PUBLISHING it**, and the only public surface mentioning supporter features still badges them *Coming Soon* with a waitlist button while `/help` is authentication-gated with no supporter section — **neither is engineering scope, and Phase 1 has no audience until both are fixed.** ⚠️ **`NoteVisibility` stays `PRIVATE \| PUBLIC`; a `SHARED` enum value is REJECTED** — access is owner-scoped in every read path so the enum grants nobody anything, and `AccountPurgeService` retains `PUBLIC` and deletes `PRIVATE`, so a `SHARED` note would match neither branch and survive the purge of a deleted account while staying readable. ⚠️ **Phases are SEQUENCED, NOT evidence-gated** (owner, 2026-08-27): with zero connections in production a gating read returns nothing, so gating would be an indefinite stop. ⚠️ **Authorization is moving from role-directional to permission-directional** — `requireAcceptedLearnerId` hardcodes caller-is-supporter, which Phase 3 replaces with per-direction, per-scope grants; guardian consent must be re-asserted inside that check or `v0.89.1`'s gate quietly reopens. | **Phases 1 and 2 are RELEASED** (`v0.91.0`, `v0.92.0`, both signed off 2026-08-27): `note_shares` and controlled material sharing, then the `linked_learner_grants` table with **both** scopes but using only `ACTIVITY`, a `requireGrant` check with guardian consent re-asserted inside it, directional opt-in UI, and a momentum view. **Phases 1, 2 and 3 are RELEASED** (`v0.91.0`, `v0.92.0`, `v0.93.0`). Phase 3 shipped 2026-08-28: it reads the `PROGRESS` scope `V125` already ships, reimplements `requireAcceptedLearnerId` over `requireGrant(..., PROGRESS)`, adds the per-scope permission UI and a progress grant→view funnel — **one migration, not two, as planned; this release adds none.** **⚠️ Re-verified against code at the `v0.93.0` kickoff, and it is why Phase 3 was chosen over any other candidate:** `LinkedLearnerProgressService.getProgress` gates on `requireAcceptedLearnerId` alone — no grant check — and returns `getStudyEngagement(learnerUserId)` verbatim, **the identical four fields Phase 2 put behind a grant**, plus a superset, so the Phase 2 activity toggle does not bound what a supporter sees. **⚠️ The reimplementation's trap, recorded here so it is not rediscovered:** `requireGrant` returns the *other party* — the **supporter** when the caller is a learner — so a thin passthrough would silently ship bidirectional progress; §4's per-direction table describes the grant **row**, not the endpoint, and **the read stays unidirectional**. **⚠️ After `v0.93.0`, an `ACCEPTED` relationship no longer implies progress access**, and **no backfill grants `PROGRESS` to existing accepted relationships** — free while production is empty, forbidden anyway because it converts an implicit rule into explicit consent nobody gave. **Phase 4 is RELEASED but PARTIAL** (`v0.94.0`, 2026-08-29): shareable single-use invitation links and connection management shipped; **supporter onboarding did NOT**, because onboarding was frozen until `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]`. That freeze lifts imminently, making supporter onboarding the natural `v0.95.0` — it is the only named Phase 4 item still outstanding. **⚠️ Link redemption creates `PENDING` and the CREATOR confirms**, because with a link the inviter names nobody and redemption alone would be one party agreeing twice; do not 'simplify' that to direct acceptance. **⚠️ Carried into Phase 5's decision, not a defect:** an unconfirmed redemption permanently writes the redeemer's write-once birth year and discloses their name and minor-status to the creator — widened from the email path, which required already knowing an address. **Phase 5 (motivation experiments) remains uncommitted; Phase 5 (motivation experiments) remains uncommitted** — rings, leaderboards and reactions each need their own decision. **⚠️ Carried into Phase 4 as the top recorded limitation:** `LinkedLearnerProgressResponse.engagement` is byte-for-byte the payload the `ACTIVITY` scope gates, so a live `PROGRESS` grant makes the activity toggle INERT, and the learner is not told — found independently by two cold agents from opposite directions at the `v0.93.0` pressure test. It is a disclosure defect, not an access one, and the honest fix (couple the toggles, or drop `engagement` from the progress payload and require both grants) is a product decision `v0.93.0`'s own anti-drift rule forbade it from taking. Neither Phase 2 nor Phase 3 adds any measurement: `MEANINGFUL_STUDY_ACTIVITIES` already defines what *studied* means (excluding `OPENED_STUDY_PACK`), and Phase 3 permissions an existing payload without adding a field. | none — Phase 4 is sequenced, not gated | none — Phase 5 is uncommitted, and supporter onboarding unblocks at 2026-09-11 | 2026-08-29 |
| **Support Another Learner — helping someone else learn, as a capability rather than a Profile Type** | Owner direction ratified 2026-08-14, revising a same-day "Guardian profile" draft that modelled it as a new `ProfileType`. Proposal: `docs/claude-plans/support-another-learner-proposal.md`. Surfacing ratification: `docs/claude-plans/learning-companion-surfacing-product-ux-consultation-prompt.md` (written and answered 2026-08-20 — **a standing decision a future re-proposal must read, not a spent artifact**) | **Direction ratified, scope NOT ratified.** The job is *help someone else learn* — a parent, grandparent, sibling, tutor, mentor or coach — not one relationship. **`ProfileType` answers "how do YOU learn?"; supporting someone answers "who else are you helping?"** Those stay independent axes, the same correction `ADR-001` made for `course_program`; a supporter may therefore also be a learner, which an enum value forbids. **Ratified rollout is three phases:** (1) help another learner — no shared subscription, no shared quota, no dashboards, **no permissions model**; (2) linked learners, invitation and acceptance; (3) progress sharing. **Phase 3 is where the product's first cross-user read lives**, so Phases 1–2 ship without touching authorization at all. **Ratified privacy line: a supporter sees readiness, progress and quiz performance, NEVER the learner's personal notes** — notes are personal thinking, progress is educational, and the line also protects the core loop, since learners who suspect notes are visible write less honestly. **Ratified: no per-learner charge** — the link is metadata and costs nothing; each learner keeps their own account and quota | **✅ SURFACING RATIFIED 2026-08-20, after a product-UX consultation was written and the owner answered it directly.** **Option A (an opt-in toggle) REJECTED — Learning Connections stays a CAPABILITY, never a profile mode or an opt-in mode**; a preference would re-introduce the very mode the capability framing exists to remove. **"Quiz for someone" leaves the Study Pack practice row entirely** — it is a support/share action, not learner practice, and does not belong beside Start Quick Review, where it also risks becoming an avoidance path on the surface retention is decided. It moves to a secondary **note action / share** surface. **⚠️ CORRECTION TO THE SESSION'S OWN PROPOSAL: quiz sharing is NOT gated on having an accepted connection.** That option coupled two independent things — **a shared-quiz recipient needs no account and no relationship**, so sharing cannot depend on one existing. **Accepted connections gate supporter-specific surfaces ONLY** (People You Support, progress); they never determine whether sharing exists. **Learning Connections stays independently discoverable**, so someone with no relationship can still learn about both. **`/progress` stays focused on the CURRENT learner**; supporter progress stays under Learning Connections / Dashboard, because the two carry different privacy contracts. **⚠️ Connection invitations stay ONE-AT-A-TIME as a principle, not a deferral: sharing a quiz link IS the many-recipient mechanism, while forming an ongoing, permission-bearing relationship is personal and should stay deliberate.** The email-keyed-invitation work still stands on its own merits — it closes the account-existence oracle (**SHIPPED in `v0.90.0`**, along with invitation expiry, two-key rate limiting on the invite endpoint, and a verified-email gate on every path that grants or widens access; relationship rows are now created only at acceptance, which changed what `PENDING` means — see the `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-19]` row, whose read this alters) regardless of whether multi-recipient invites are ever built. **✅ Q4 AND Q5 ALSO RATIFIED 2026-08-19, which unblocks Phase 2 scoping.** **Q4 — linking is invite + accept in both directions, revocable from either side.** Acceptance is the load-bearing half: an opt-out notification would leave the relationship existing, and progress potentially visible, before the learner agreed to anything. Learner-initiated-code-only was rejected — strictest, but it requires the learner to act first, failing exactly where the parent→young-child case needs it most. **Q5 — age is collected AT LINK TIME, never at signup, with a guardian consent step below the threshold.** Collecting at link time keeps onboarding untouched, which matters concretely because `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]` is measuring it against a 62.4% baseline. **⚠️ `learnerLevel` was considered as an age proxy and REJECTED** — it implies minors for `GRADE_SCHOOL`/`JUNIOR_HIGH`/`SENIOR_HIGH` and costs nothing, but the mapping breaks both ways (`PERSONAL_LEARNING`, adult re-takers) and a proxy is weaker ground than a declared age. Adults-only-first was rejected because it excludes the motivating parent→child case. **⚠️ THE AGE THRESHOLD ITSELF IS NOT DECIDED AND MUST NOT BE GUESSED IN SCOPING** — the mechanism is ratified, the number is a legal question under the Philippine Data Privacy Act and is owner-owned pending counsel. Build the threshold as configuration, not a literal, and ship no default that reads as a legal position. **✅ THE DECISION GATE IS CLEARED — audit run 2026-08-19, and Phase 1 SCOPED INTO `v0.89.0`.** The answer to *does Phase 1 already exist* is **yes, almost entirely**: generating a quiz for another person is ungated, `/quiz/share/**` is `permitAll` so the recipient needs no account, and plan metering already exists. **One gate hides all of it — `QuizShareLinkService.requireTeacherOrAdmin:160-166`** — and it is an axis error, not a missing feature: a parent helping a child is a `STUDENT`/`BOARD_EXAM` profile and must misrepresent their own profile to unlock it. **⚠️ The audit also DISSOLVED three of this row's other blockers for Phase 1 specifically**, because Phase 1 records no relationship: consent has nothing to consent to, minors/DPA store nothing about the recipient, and *whose quota* is already answered by the existing code (the supporter's own account and own share limit). **They all return from Phase 2**, when a relationship is recorded. **✅ ALL THREE PHASES SHIPPED, `v0.89.0`–`v0.94.0` — VERIFIED STALE AND CORRECTED AT THE `v0.143.0` KICKOFF, 2026-09-11.** The `[DECISION]+[EVIDENCE]` text below this point described the pre-decision state and was left trailing after the decision that resolved it, reading as still-open. `requireTeacherOrAdmin` was removed in commit `cbc7d13c` (`v0.89.0`, 2026-08-19, "feat: let any onboarded user share a generated quiz") — confirmed absent from `QuizShareLinkService.java` today. Phase 2 (linked-learner relationships, mutual invite/accept, age-at-link-time + guardian consent) and Phase 3 (supporter-scoped progress read) shipped `v0.90.0`–`v0.94.0`. The surfacing relocation is also built: `private-note-detail-page-client.tsx:2548-2568` has a "Quiz for someone" note-action-menu item gated only on `!isTeacherMode`, with a comment quoting the 2026-08-20 ratification verbatim. **Fresh production read, 2026-09-11: 0 `PARENT`-profile users (still correctly unwired); 1 non-teacher quiz share link from 1 distinct STUDENT/BOARD_EXAM user — small but real, non-hypothetical usage.** **⚠️ THIS ROW DUPLICATES "Learning Connections — ratified five-phase direction" BELOW — same shipped arc, different name/phase numbering, almost certainly left behind by a renaming.** Whatever remains outstanding under either name (supporter onboarding) is the one item already tracked and deferred to `v0.144.0` on that row, not new scope here. Original pre-decision gate text, kept for its reasoning: *does Phase 1 already largely exist? Teachers already generate quizzes for others via `generatedQuiz`, and shareable quiz links already work — so Phase 1 may be a packaging and discoverability problem rather than a build.* | 2026-09-11 |
| **Adaptive Practice becomes the recommendation engine, not a post-quiz next step** | Owner direction ratified 2026-08-14. Full direction, findings and open questions: `docs/claude-plans/adaptive-practice-recommendation-engine.md` | **FIRST SLICE SHIPPED as `v0.77.0` (2026-08-14):** the Dashboard's weak-concept focus now resolves from `ConceptHealth`'s `incorrect_streak` against the existing `TWICE_MISSED_STREAK_THRESHOLD` instead of the single latest completed Quick Review, so one bad quiz no longer produces a recommendation. **Within-pack only** — cross-pack still needs canonical concept identity. **No entry point was removed**, so the 2026-09-12 constraint is untouched. Philosophy ratified, remaining scope NOT ratified. Adaptive Practice should appear because a learner has demonstrated a **persistent** weakness across multiple assessments — not because they just finished a quiz. Primarily **dashboard-driven** (Today's Focus / Weak Concepts), with Challenge Quiz completion celebrating the quiz instead of offering remediation. Target feeling: *"the system noticed something I keep struggling with"*, not *"the system wants me to take another quiz"*. **This is the first mechanism that would make "Always know what to learn next" literally true.** Smaller than it sounds in one respect: the Dashboard already routes to Adaptive Practice from its due- and weak-concept branches, so the change is **raising the evidence bar and making it the primary route**, not adding a surface | **`[DECISION]` — and it carries a HARD SEQUENCING CONSTRAINT.** ⚠️ **Do not remove the Challenge Quiz entry point before 2026-09-12.** `v0.74.0` already removed Adaptive Practice from the Quick Review result screen, and the open `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-12]` measures exactly that; a second removal confounds the read rather than answering it. **Worse, that checkpoint's kill criterion says to "restore a demoted entry point" if adoption falls — which directly contradicts this direction**, so the checkpoint ~~must be re-specified now (remedy becomes *accelerate the dashboard recommendation*), or it will fire in September and someone will correctly follow an instruction that undoes a ratified direction.~~ **✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-14 — the remedy WAS re-specified, and the checkpoint row carries it struck through. ⚠️ THIS WARNING IS STALE AND IS STRUCK THROUGH RATHER THAN DELETED, because it caused a REAL wrong scoping decision at the `v0.107.0` kickoff (2026-09-03), which scoped an already-completed re-specification as new work.** Two rows described one obligation — this one carried the warning, the checkpoint row carried the completed action — and the warning row was read first. **A future reader needs the discharge visible HERE, on the row that misled, not only on the row that was right.** **Architecture finding: the missing layer is CONCEPT IDENTITY, not just a learning signal.** `ConceptHealthEntity` already stores `incorrect_streak`, so within-pack repeated evidence is **available today** — but `concept` is a free-text string keyed per Study Pack, so cross-pack recommendation (*"this keeps coming back everywhere"*) needs canonical concepts first, the same problem `ADR-001` solved for programs. **Within-pack and cross-pack differ by roughly an order of magnitude in cost; decide which is meant first.** **Second fork:** this either justifies consolidating the **8 independent "what's next" resolvers** the Companion Guidance Doctrine pressure test found, or becomes the 9th — and that doctrine's blocking Phase 0 (Primary-Review-Set vs Study/Exam-Focus) probably cannot be dodged by a dashboard-first engine. **Product tension:** Adaptive Practice is capped at 3/month on Free, so a higher evidence bar may mean the tier most likely to convert never sees a recommendation at all | 2026-08-14 |
| **Onboarding Intent Router — onboarding as an intelligent router, not profile collection** | `v0.71.0` pre-signoff pressure test (`docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md`), which found onboarding the flow most affected by ADR-001 Release B. Full plan: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-activation-and-intent-router.md`. Production audit queries: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-review-set-vocabulary-audit.sql` | **Scoped into `v0.71.0` as slice 5 — owner ruling 2026-08-06.** Added after kickoff, deliberately. Trigger was **B0**, an activation-blocking regression this release introduced: `resolveRequestedCourseProgram` gained a `throw` where `main` returned null, while onboarding creates its first note without sending a program — so a new user gets *"Choose at least one course or program."* on a screen with no such field and cannot complete onboarding. **Owner-ratified governing philosophy: onboarding is no longer profile collection — it is an intelligent router that helps every learner reach the fastest successful study experience available to them.** This generalizes Phase 1's practice-first branch (below) from a `BOARD_EXAM` carve-out to every profile type, with a first-intent step whose copy resolves against the learner's own program *before* selection, and an honest availability state for unsupported programs | **GATE FULLY CLEARED 2026-08-06 — the production audit ran and came back clean.** All four published Official Review Sets are tagged with **exact catalog names** (Accountancy, Architecture, Education, Nursing); no exam slugs, no case drift, no mixed conventions, so **no resolution layer is needed** and building one would be the unnecessary normalization the audit was commissioned to avoid. The Intent Router is unblocked. Results: `docs/claude-plans/onboarding-review-set-vocabulary-audit-results.md`. **Two findings from the audit that change the design weighting:** (a) coverage is far better than the program count suggests — the four covered programs hold **179 of 218 program-holding accounts (82.1%)**, so Branch A serves most users and the "Coming soon" state is a ~18% minority path, correcting an earlier reading that argued from 4-of-21 programs; (b) **38.7% of accounts (141/364) still never complete onboarding**, closely tracking the ~40% recorded 2026-07-28 by an independent path — this is the pre-change baseline to re-measure against, and the audit also confirmed the fire-and-forget context write has really bitten users (5 completed with no course program, 4 with no learner level). Residual, not blocking: production tagging is clean but unguarded — the admin publish picker draws options from public **note** values rather than the catalog, so the recommended slice-5 guard is to repoint that picker, not to normalize the match path. Why the owner overrode deferral: onboarding is a one-time flow, so unlike slice 4 there is no re-learning cost — the decision rests on not shipping a first impression that models the wrong product. **Slice 5 shipped in `v0.71.0`; C8 and C9 did NOT close and were re-recorded at signoff 2026-08-10** per the rule in `RELEASES.md` that they be re-recorded rather than closed if slice 5 did not cover them. **✅ BOTH C8 AND C9 SHIPPED IN COMMIT `826ca155`, 2026-08-12 ("feat: close C8 and C9, the carried Intent Router residuals") — VERIFIED STALE AND CORRECTED AT THE `v0.143.0` KICKOFF, 2026-09-11, five weeks after they closed.** This row was offered as a live candidate before the fix was found already shipped. Verified against current code: `frontend/lib/onboarding-v2.ts:254`'s `getCourseProgramScreenCopy` gives TEACHER distinct copy ("What do you teach?", placeholder `e.g. BS Civil Engineering`) from STUDENT/BOARD_EXAM/PROFESSIONAL — C9's byte-identical-copy claim is false today. `applicable-programs-combobox.tsx:316` now renders an explanatory message naming the off-catalog profile program instead of a bare "No course programs selected." — C8's claim is false today. Original findings kept below for their reasoning, not as live status. **C9's stated blocker expired but the finding is untouched (AS OF 2026-08-10, NOW STALE):** slice 5 moved `profileType` to Step 1, so Step 2 now knows it is talking to a curator, yet its Course / Program copy is still byte-identical for all four profile types — a teacher is still asked for a free-text program that can never be a note program under the two-mode model. It should be re-triaged on effort, not carried as blocked. **C8 is half-fixed, and the remaining half is what C8 was actually about (AS OF 2026-08-10, NOW STALE):** C5 stopped the editor contradicting itself (no more *"Tailored for: X"* over zero selected programs), but §3's authoring-time affordance did not ship — a curator whose profile program is off-catalog still gets bare *"No course programs selected."* with no explanation, asserted by a passing test in `note-editor-page-client.test.tsx`. **Considered and NOT taken as `v0.72.1`'s build half, 2026-08-11 — and the reason matters, because it is not a demotion.** The activation read measured the cost of this surface precisely: **132 users, 35.2% of all signups**, verify their email and then never complete onboarding, making it by far the largest single leak in the funnel. It was not taken because recovering that leak is *not the fastest path to returning users* — activation is already 52.2%, which structurally caps the volume lever below the retention lever — **not** because the findings are weak or the cost is small. **Do not re-read this row as deprioritized on merit.** What the read removed is one specific justification: these can no longer be scoped *as a retention intervention*. They remain fully live on their own terms. **Also recorded: the read could not locate where *within* onboarding the 132 drop** — `profile_type` is only persisted at onboarding's final step, so the `users` table cannot distinguish a step-1 drop from a step-4 one, and the event-based read has n=5. Anyone scoping this should expect to answer that first | 2026-09-11 |
| **`v0.71.1` — four architectural deferrals from the `v0.71.0` signoff pressure test** | `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-signoff-pressure-test.md` — **⚠️ PROPOSED FOR DELETION AT THE `v0.97.0` KICKOFF AND KEPT, 2026-08-29.** The four deferrals did all ship, but the file also carries **Frontend #4, explicitly deferred and still carried**: the deferred-completion marker is never retried, `completeOnboardingAndLeave` is a new call site that increases exposure, and **that exit is the only terminal one that does not `clearOnboardingDraft`**. This row's Status column preserves one line of that; the file holds the mechanism, the amplifier and the verification that it does not affect the Diagnostic Read. **Unresolved, so it stays under the ruled principle.** |  **Deferred at signoff 2026-08-10 with an explicit owner ruling** (*"fix everything except the four, defer the other four"*), not dropped. They share one cause: `V108` stops the *migration* mechanically deriving a learner's personal program into a catalog applicability row, but nothing stopped the same state arising afterwards. **(1)** `NoteApplicableProgramsService.replace` recreated the `V108` class by ordinary admin action — an admin could set catalog programs on any learner's note and a learner update never clears them; **(2)** a learner saw inherited programs on a copied curated note they could not edit, and was forced to fill a required Course / Program whose value had no visible effect; **(3)** `NoteBulkGenerationService` still used the bare curator predicate corrected elsewhere in the release; **(4)** AI-suggestion apply dead-ended on a copy of a curated note for accounts with a null profile program | **SCOPED INTO `v0.71.1` at kickoff 2026-08-10. ALL FOUR ARE NOW SHIPPED — Group 1 is complete (2026-08-11).** **(3) shipped 2026-08-11:** the curator onboarding guard now covers all three authoring paths, and `CLAUDE.md` no longer names an in-repo exception. **(1) shipped 2026-08-11** — `ADR-001` → *Curation authority*: a row may only be authored onto a note its author owns. **Ownership rather than visibility, because the public→private flip makes a write-moment gate insufficient** — a learner can flip a curated public note back to private, so gating the write does not constrain the resulting state, and closing that would need the `source` column the ADR rejected. Production sizing returned **0** for the admin-authored shape and **no** non-admin-owned note carrying any join row, so prevention was free and cost nothing; no cleanup migration. Ships with the deliberately asymmetric admin-or-owner **read** path preserved and an owner-scoped Admin Dashboard. **(2) and its folded (4) shipped 2026-08-10** (PR #1019). **Do not treat `V108` as having closed these classes**, and **CLOSED at `v0.71.1` signoff, 2026-08-11** — all four items are fixed, none was re-recorded, and the pressure test still owes it a whole-release read since items 1 and 2 both landed in `NoteApplicableProgramsService` | 2026-08-11 |
| **Twenty Medium/Low findings carried from the `v0.71.0` pre-signoff pressure test** | `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md` (`file:line` for every one); listed individually in `RELEASES.md` under `v0.71.0` Known Limitations | **Carried, not dropped — owner ruling 2026-08-07** (*"this release was already huge enough"*). Each was **re-verified against current code on 2026-08-07** rather than copied forward from the pressure test. None is reachable-and-harmful enough to hold a release. Two are not patches and should not be triaged as such: **M12** (`effectiveAuthoringDomain` can return null while `effectiveCurriculumLevel` defaults to `COLLEGE`) is an open architecture question about whether `courseProgram` is guaranteed non-null after onboarding, and needs an ADR-level answer; **M16**'s disposition needs rewriting rather than reusing, because slice 5 made `OnboardingGuardService`'s exemption load-bearing in a way it was not when "no change recommended" was written | **CLOSED at `v0.71.1` signoff, 2026-08-11 — every taken item shipped; L9 carries its own row rather than closing silently with this one.** The scoping pass this row asked for ran at kickoff and deliberately did not take all twenty; everything it took is now done, including **L4's EXPLAIN, which the finding recorded as never run** — it shows the slug fallback needs an *expression* index rather than the plain one proposed, measured locally only, so it is recorded rather than shipped. Taken: M1, M2, M10 (contracts that lie about themselves), M3, M4 (consumers still blind to the join), M11, L1, L3 (copy and control defects — **L12 moved into group 1 item 2** on 2026-08-10, since the read-only names block that ruling requires *is* its fix), and L4, L5 if room remains. **Not taken, and each for a stated reason:** L8 is not a free cleanup — the only obvious fix is a `DROP COLUMN`, which `v0.71.0` declined on irreversibility grounds and `v0.71.1`'s own additive-migrations rule forbids; M13, M15, M16 go with C8/C9 to the Onboarding Intent Router row — same vocabulary and guard question in five forms, and `v0.71.0` already ruled that constraining onboarding to the catalog is the wrong fix; **`[DECISION]`** M12 (ADR-level) and M9 (needs a reconciliation strategy) may be *answered* in `v0.71.1` but may not be patched around; L6 is not UI-reachable and L7 matches 0 notes, so neither earns a change. Also still carried, in neither group: the deferred-completion marker is never retried — pre-existing, but `v0.71.0` added a new call site | 2026-08-11 |
| **One canonical Course / Program catalog — proposed `ADR-001` amendment removing learner free-text** | `docs/claude-plans/course-program-canonical-catalog-proposal.md`, raised by the owner 2026-08-11 while scoping `v0.71.2` | **PROPOSED, not ratified.** Would amend `ADR-001`'s two-mode model so learners select from the catalog rather than typing, with a *Request Program* action where theirs is missing. **The audit found the premise is half-true and the harm is narrower than stated:** four of the six "core dimensions" are already catalog-only, and the real leak is `NoteService.listPublicCoursePrograms:1213-1221`, which unions legacy free-text from *public* notes into the public filter vocabulary. Measured: **4,480** private learner notes rely on free text against a **21-row** catalog, while only **4** non-admin public notes exist — so it constrains 4,480 notes to clean a surface currently four notes wide. **Counter-proposal recorded:** return catalog names only from the public vocabulary and make suggestions catalog-first, which fixes the verified harm in one method with no migration **✅ EVIDENCE HALF ANSWERED 2026-08-19 — and it says DECLINE, on grounds neither the proposal nor the query anticipated.** Read: `docs/claude-plans/course-program-fragmentation-read.sql`. **The amendment's premise has essentially no production footprint: learners have written 4,737 PRIVATE notes and FOUR public ones, zero of them off-catalog.** The thing it exists to prevent — learner free-text fragmenting community content — is four notes. **The off-catalog population is not drift, and it is not learners':** of 63 off-catalog notes, **48 are curator-authored**, and **42 (67%) are academic LEVELS used as programs** — `Junior High` (24), `High School` (15), `Grade School` (3). That is a category error, not free-text drift; **locking the field would not fix it, the author would simply pick a wrong program instead.** The genuine remainder is ~14 notes wanting catalog entries (`Civil Service` 7, `Software Engineering` 4, `Engineering` 2, `Biology` 1) — and `v0.71.2` already made adding those possible **without a migration**, which is the cheaper answer the amendment would foreclose. Plus `Professional / Board Exam Review` (7, all private), the known study-context catch-all. **⚠️ The pre-committed normaliser hypothesis was FALSIFIED, not confirmed:** part 5 returned **zero** casing/whitespace near-misses, so a normaliser has nothing to normalise either. Accounts sit at 33/234 = **14.1% off-catalog against `v0.79.0`'s 13.9% baseline — unchanged**, exactly as predicted for the deliberate onboarding exclusion, and therefore still not evidence for locking. **The actionable finding is a DIFFERENT row: academic levels as Course / Program**, now sized at 42 notes, 37 of them public and reachable in discovery.  | **`[DECISION]` + `[EVIDENCE]`.** Decision: is prospective community-content fragmentation worth a migration now, given Trust → Habit → Community puts that content last? Evidence that would settle it: a production read of distinct `course_program` values on public notes and across accounts. **⚠️ THAT READ IS NOW WRITTEN AND UNRUN — `docs/claude-plans/course-program-fragmentation-read.sql` (2026-08-19).** Five parts, smoke-tested for syntax. **It is built to argue AGAINST itself:** it splits curator from learner notes (900+ curator notes would otherwise drown the signal), splits public from private (only public mints a discovery facet), and part 5 isolates values that are off-catalog *only* by casing or spacing — **if those are most of the off-catalog population, the answer is a normaliser, not a locked field, and this amendment should stay declined.** Part 3 also re-surfaces academic levels used as programs, which is its own row. **⚠️ A high off-catalog rate on ACCOUNTS is expected and is NOT evidence for locking** — it is the known, deliberate cost of excluding onboarding from `v0.79.0`, itself gated on `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-11]`. **Two traps if taken:** `resolveRequestedCourseProgram` currently throws when both note and profile values are null, so every learner off the 21-row catalog would be unable to save their next edit; and at a curator headcount of one, a blocking *Request Program* makes note creation depend on the owner's response time — the B0 shape, slower. **Not `v0.71.2`** — that release is correct under either outcome | 2026-08-19 |
| **Program Family management — no way to create a family, or change its membership, without a migration** | Noticed by the owner 2026-08-11 while using the new catalog form: the Program Family dropdown offers only *No family* and *Engineering* | **Gap confirmed, deliberately not urgent.** `V106` seeds exactly one family (`Engineering`, 3 members) and no later migration adds any, so a migration is the only way to create one — the same shape as the catalog gap `v0.71.2` just closed for programs. **`v0.71.2` scoped family creation out on purpose.** It is *not* urgent because `ADR-001` limits a family to one job — *"reduce repetitive authoring work"* — so a family only earns its place when an author repeatedly selects the same set. Creating e.g. a *Health Sciences* family before any medical notes are authored is the same premature expansion `ADR-001:74` rejects for programs: a family that expands to 5 programs when the usual case is 2 costs more clicks than it saves, since the author then trims three | **`[EFFORT]`, demand-gated.** The trigger is an author noticing they have selected the same program set several times running — not a taxonomy judgement that two programs feel related. **Two constraints for whoever builds it, recorded so they are not rediscovered:** (1) the family picker must read `program_families` **directly** rather than deriving distinct families from programs the way `admin-course-program-catalog-section.tsx:27-31` does today, or a newly created family is invisible until something already uses it — you could not assign the family you just made; (2) **membership is the real lever, not creation** — `ADR-001` says *"if a family should expand to a different set, that is a change to membership"*, so the surface needs add/remove-member, otherwise you get families you cannot correct | 2026-08-11 |
| **L9 — `notes.status` nullability differs across test schemas sharing a JVM-lifetime H2** | `docs/claude-findings/v0.71.0-pre-signoff-pressure-test.md:639`; carried from `v0.71.0` Known Limitations | **Not taken in `v0.71.1`, with a reason — added 2026-08-11 when the pressure test found it had no disposition anywhere.** It was the only one of the twenty carried Medium/Low findings the triage never mentioned in either direction, and the Backlog row for that set was about to close declaring all twenty handled. `notes.status` is `not null` in three test schemas and nullable in two, over a shared H2 using `create table if not exists`, so the first schema to load wins and the others silently inherit it | **`[EFFORT]`** — the fix is consolidating the schemas behind one definition, which changes how every integration test boots. Not a patch, and not something to land mid-release: the suite is currently green, and the risk is order-dependent future breakage rather than anything user-facing. Un-parked by a test-infrastructure pass | 2026-08-11 |
| **Overlapping representations on a learner's copy — the bug class `v0.71.1` patches the symptom of** | `ADR-001` → *"No learner-facing Applicable Programs UI" governs authoring, not provenance display* (ratified 2026-08-10), which closes the symptom and explicitly leaves this open | **Deferred at the `v0.71.1` learner-visible-programs ruling, 2026-08-10.** A curated note copied by a learner carries **both** representations — `notes.course_program` "as copied" plus inherited join rows — while read semantics make the join rows win whenever they exist. The string is therefore unreadable on that shape, and `v0.71.1` fixes that by no longer requiring it and by showing the inherited programs read-only. **That is symptom treatment: the two representations still overlap, and every future surface reading either one inherits the ambiguity.** Two options close the class outright — **(D)** stop inheriting join rows on copy, so a learner's copy becomes a personal-string note; **(C)** let a learner's own value clear the inherited rows on their copy. **Sharpest evidence for eventually taking one:** `canManageVisibility` lets a learner flip their copy to `PUBLIC`, and public discovery is join-first, so inherited curator rows can drive **public** shelves for a note the curator never published. Default-private is not stays-private | **`[DECISION]`** — both options amend ratified `ADR-001` text (D contradicts the canonical-shapes table's third row; C is the learner-save synchronization the representation-authority ruling rejected, though that rejection's stated reason — cannot distinguish derived from inherited rows — does not apply on a copy, where every row is inherited by construction). **Not a patch-release decision**; do not fold into `v0.71.1`. Un-parked by an owner call on whether a learner's copy should keep curator applicability at all, which is a product question about what a copy *is*, not a data question | 2026-08-10 |
| **Single Program Axis — remove "Primary Course / Program"** | Owner architectural review 2026-08-05, after Release B's two-field model met real use. Scoping view in the "Single Program Axis" section above; binding text in `ADR-001` → "Course / Program(s) is the only program concept". GPT consultation prompt: `22-remove-primary-program-gpt-consultation-prompt.md` | **Scoped into `v0.71.0` as slice 4 — owner ruling 2026-08-05.** Added after kickoff, deliberately: it *simplifies* what this release built rather than adding scope, and both things that make it free expire at signoff | **Gate cleared.** Sequenced ahead of signoff because signoff *is* deploy, and deploy is what makes it expensive: teachers would learn the two-field model and then re-learn it, and curators could create multi-program notes needing a Domain Context backfill. It also means the whole-release pressure test runs **once**, against the final architecture, instead of against an intermediate one | 2026-08-05 |
| **~~Retire the frozen `notes.course_program` compatibility column~~ — WITHDRAWN 2026-08-05** | `ADR-001` → "Two authoring modes — and therefore two program fields" | **Withdrawn the same day it was opened, and the reasoning is worth keeping.** This row was created on the premise that a catalog-only picker would close the tap, making the off-catalog population finite and the column retirable. The owner then ruled that **personal notes keep free-text programs** — NoteLib is a notebook, not a closed LMS — which reopens the tap permanently *and reveals the column was never legacy*. It is **the personal-notes program field**; `note_course_program` is the curated-content axis. Slice 2's "legacy fallback" turns out to describe the mode boundary, not a compatibility shim. Retiring the column would delete personal-note discovery | **None — there is nothing to un-park.** Query A ran against production 2026-08-06 and is recorded in `25-query-a-production-results.md`: **56 of 4,787 notes (1.17%) sit outside the catalog, and every one is a deliberate prior exclusion** — 37 of them bare school levels `V104`/`V105` reclassified. So the fallback is a small, well-understood, permanent surface rather than data debt. The `legacy_course_program` rename stays dropped: renaming a current field to "legacy" would be actively misleading | 2026-08-06 |
| **"Publishing Settings" as the curator mental model** | Owner observation 2026-08-05, during the slice 4 authoring-UX pass | **Direction only, deliberately unscoped.** For Teacher/Admin, Subject, Course / Program(s), Domain Context, Note Learner Level, Visibility, and Review Set membership are starting to describe a **publishing workflow** rather than ordinary note metadata, and grouping them as "Metadata" undersells what they do. The two-authoring-modes distinction ratified in `ADR-001` is the first concrete step toward this — once the modes are named, the grouping question gets much easier, because "publishing settings" is simply what curation *is* | **[DECISION]** — needs a scoping pass of its own, and it should follow real use of the slice 4 authoring surfaces rather than preceding it. Not a `v0.71.0` item | 2026-08-05 |
| **Canonical Knowledge Architecture (Applicable Programs / Domain Context / Note Learner Level)** | Owner + product-UX GPT discussion relayed 2026-08-03, prompted by hitting the single-valued `course_program` wall while expanding the Official Library into Civil Engineering. Full critique, code audit (59 backend + 40 frontend non-test files), 14-item migration inventory, and 4-step sequencing in `docs/claude-prompt/canonical-knowledge-architecture-out/01-architecture-critique-and-migration-plan.md`; draft ADR in `docs/architecture/ADR-001-canonical-knowledge-architecture.md`; production vocabulary audit queries in `03`/`04` with results and revisions in `05-vocabulary-results.md`; proposed Domain Context taxonomy in `06-domain-context-taxonomy.md`; product-UX-GPT consultation prompt in `07`; **Query K validation + answers to the seven governance questions in `08-taxonomy-validation-and-final-recommendation.md` — read `06` through `08`, which invalidates part of it** | **Planned, not ratified — decomposed into 4 independently-gated steps.** Audit found the direction right and the code evidence stronger than the proposal's own: `OpenAiLlmStudyPackService:1536-1537`'s "treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain … Do not blend in material from unrelated disciplines" is logically unsatisfiable under many-valued programs, making Domain Context forced rather than optional. Three corrections to the proposal: (1) it is 3 initiatives with non-comparable cost/reversibility, and **Domain Context alone unblocks canonical authoring** because Review Sets already compose notes freely by explicit reference — so comprehensive Official Review Sets do not wait on the join table; (2) the migration premise is wrong — there is no `course_programs` table (`V39__note_course_program.sql` adds a plain `VARCHAR(120)` column despite its name), `UpsertNoteRequest.courseProgram` has no validation, and an LLM suggestion can write into the field (`StudyPackService:746`), so the backfill is vocabulary reconciliation with editorial judgment calls, not `INSERT … SELECT`; (3) Note Learner Level *reverses* an explicit current rule (`:1540-1541` "Do not use learner level to calibrate static note or Study Pack content" — static depth is set by course/program today and learner level is deliberately excluded) and requires re-keying `exam_question_pool.learner_level` + `challenge_quiz_question_bank.learner_level`, both of which key on **user** level today via `ExamQuestionPoolService.sameLearnerLevel()`. Also surfaced, none of it in the proposal: `notes.target_profile_type` (`NoteTargetProfileType`, NOT NULL) is an undiscussed fourth axis — checked, and it is **not** write-only: it is a live Public Library audience filter (`PublicLibraryRepositoryImpl:176-178`, `NoteController:594`/`:636`, `NoteRepository:106`/`:131`), so it sits on the *discovery* axis alongside Applicable Programs rather than conflicting with Note Learner Level; the real question it raises is whether precise program facets make a coarse 3-value audience facet redundant, judged at the end of step 3, not now; `getExamSlugForCourseProgram` returns exactly one slug via `.find()`, so many-valued programs make Exam Hub resolution config-order-dependent; `isQuantitativeContext` would be diluted by a joined program list; Review Set course/program is behavioral (`NoteCollectionService:175` public filter, `:1231` hierarchy cascade) and can now legitimately diverge from its notes; personal copies must not inherit Applicable Programs (`NoteService:264`). **Step 3 fails bootstrap-test clause 2** (not withdrawable without a migration once filters/badges read the join) — recorded as a knowing multi-release commitment, not an oversight. Does **not** supersede Company Redefinition Phase 3b: this ADR makes knowledge canonical, not generated artifacts — pools stay per-`study_pack_id` | **[DECISION] — FULLY RATIFIED 2026-08-03. No gates and no open decisions remain; Release A is ready to `/kickoff`.** Ratified in a second pass the same day: **(1)** the **8-value Domain Context set** — `Engineering Mathematics`, `Engineering Sciences`, `Civil Engineering`, `Professional Practice & Regulation`, `General Education`, `Professional Education`, `Nursing`, `Accountancy`; **(2)** **`Architecture` deliberately gets NO Domain Context** despite 837 notes — it uses the program-name fallback until shared canonical knowledge accumulates, because duplication reduction and not note volume is what earns a context (the clearest worked example of the governance rule); **(3)** the axis is renamed **Content Context → Domain Context** (`notes.domain_context`) before the migration — it names the field's actual job (feeding the prompt's "authoritative academic **domain**" line) and avoids a semantic collision with the pre-existing `OpenAiLlmStudyPackService.buildContentContextBlock` (`:1523`), which already means the static-content variant of the context block; **(4)** the success metric is restated as *"Eliminates duplication of shared knowledge as NoteLib expands to more programs"* — comprehensive Review Sets, faster expansion, and lower maintenance are downstream benefits, not the guarantee; **(5)** governance now states that **Domain Contexts are expected to remain relatively stable and adding one is an architectural decision, not routine curriculum authoring** ("adding notes is authoring; adding a Domain Context is architecture"). **The ADR is Accepted and promoted to `docs/architecture/ADR-001-canonical-knowledge-architecture.md`** — the repo's first ADR, establishing the `docs/architecture/ADR-NNN-*.md` convention. Superseded history: the one decision this row previously listed as open was **the Domain Context value set** — proposed in `06-domain-context-taxonomy.md` (10 values: Tier 1 `Engineering Mathematics` / `Engineering Sciences` / `Civil Engineering` to unblock the Algebra work, Tier 2 covering existing content, Tier 3 explicitly not minted). Its rule — *Domain Context is the coarsest label under which the note's treatment is identical* — rules out the original proposal's "Engineering Foundation"-style values as **weaker domain constraints than the `Civil Engineering` they replace** (risk R4), since the value is substituted into the prompt's "treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain" line. `06` §6 carries five owner questions, the load-bearing one being whether `Engineering Sciences` is shared by all 11 engineering programs or a subset (a PRC syllabus question, not an architecture one — the subject groupings in `06` Tier 1 are **unverified against current board syllabi** and must be curator-checked, with `06` §7's Query K provided for that). **All data gates are closed.** Production vocabulary audit (`03`) and follow-ups (`04`) both run 2026-08-03; results in `05-vocabulary-results.md`. Every gate this row originally carried is now closed by data: **(a) Step 2's cost gate cleared cheaply** — 27 note-side / 16 user-side values, **32 in the union, ZERO character-level collisions**, so the feared typo-variant reconciliation does not exist and Step 2 folds into Step 1's release (~6 semantic judgment calls remain: `Bsed`→Education, the Computer Science/IT/Software Engineering overlap, `Engineering` as a family, `Biology` as a subject, `Civil Service`/`Professional / Board Exam Review`/`Self Study` as activities). **(b) Step 3's **RETIRED CHECKPOINT** (was due 2027-02-01 — deliberately written without the live-checkpoint token so the kickoff step-9 scan does not flag it) was answered affirmatively before it was needed.** **The motivating case is anticipatory, and should be quoted as the reason rather than the corroborating find below:** under the Civil Engineering Review Set's **Engineering Mathematics** subject plan, the next authoring step is creating topic notes for the **Algebra** subject; the owner stopped before creating them, because those same notes will be needed by ten further engineering programs and authoring under a single-program model commits to duplicating them ten times. The decision is "do not create the duplication in the first place," taken at the moment of authoring. **Corroborating evidence, not the reason:** Query J found duplication has independently already begun — `Strength of Materials` holds "Stress and Strain in Strength of Materials" (Civil Engineering) and "Stress, Strain, and Material Strength" (Mechanical Engineering), the same knowledge as two notes under two programs, **invisible to Baseline A's exact-title matching**. Baseline A's 0.00% must never be cited as evidence of no duplication. Nine further CE SoM notes are queued to need a twin per additional engineering program. **(c) The "maybe assembly is the real gap" reading is WITHDRAWN as wrong** — the owner confirmed the CE Review Set is mid-build and blocked by precisely this; Query I corroborates (197 CE official public notes: 94 across 4 PRIVATE collections, 1 public, 102 in none). **Steps 1+2+4 should ship as ONE release, not three**: all additive/reversible, all touching `buildGenerationContextBlock`, and **49 notes currently feed a K-12 grade level (`Junior High`/`High School`/`Grade School`/`Senior High – STEM`/`– ABM`/`– HUMSS`) into the prompt's "treat the course/program above as the authoritative academic domain" line — an active generation-quality bug today**, in the same method Step 1 already edits; its backfill is mechanical for those six values, and all 49 are in zero collections so the risk is low. Pool/bank re-keying is the one separable PR inside that release. Step 3 follows as its own multi-release effort, now justified on evidence rather than held as a bet. **§9 Baseline B restated from Query H's hierarchy rollup: 4 comprehensive Official Review Sets averaging ~58 notes (CPALE 74, PNLE 63, ALE 52, LET 43) against a target of several hundred** — the earlier "avg 8.6" was per-subject-child and must not be quoted. Two adjacent items surfaced and are explicitly NOT folded in, each needing its own row: **Bulk Generate intra-program de-duplication** (near-duplicate titles within one program, e.g. "High Alert Medications in Nursing Pharmacology" vs "…in Nursing Practice") and the **general assembly backlog** (~330 official public notes across 5 programs in no collection) | 2026-08-04 |
| Retention H1 + H5 (commitment device + pre-decided return action) | `retention-diagnosis-session-plan.md` | **SHIPPED in `v0.72.0` — signed off, merged and deployed 2026-08-11.** Was "Conditional — next up" until this kickoff; the signoff never updated it. Confirmed by `next-priority-new-user-focus-out/01-next-priority-new-user-focus.md` to fit "new users to retain" precisely (targets a user's first 1-2 weeks after signup, not lapsed/churned users) — do not conflate with the churned-user interview item below despite sharing a source doc | **⚠️⚠️ PROXIMAL `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-09-10]` — READ 2026-09-10. VERDICT: THE KILL CRITERION FIRED.** Nine learners shown the prompt 2026-08-11 → 2026-09-09; **ZERO committed**, one declined. **⚠️ THE ZERO IS REAL AND TWO INDEPENDENT INSTRUMENTS AGREE — checked FIRST, because `v0.116.0` and `v0.117.0` both shipped events that could never fire.** `COMMITTED` and `DECLINED` emit from the **same line** (`review-commitment-prompt.tsx:102`, a ternary) and `DECLINED` fired once, so the call site provably executes; independently `SELECT count(*) FROM users WHERE cardinality(review_days) > 0` = **0**, which rules out the analytics-delivery bias that made `v0.80.0` necessary. **⚠️ DENOMINATOR, HONESTLY: n = 9.** A ~10% true commit rate is not excluded by chance; ≥30% (P(zero) ≈ 4%) effectively is. **This is not proof the feature is dead.** **The rule said the framing reverts to UNCONFIRMED and is *reopened rather than iterated on with further nudge tuning*.** **⚠️ OWNER OVERRIDE 2026-09-10: the owner elected to REDESIGN THE PROMPT — the tuning that rule names — after being told so. Recorded, not smoothed over: a pre-committed rule quietly stepped over stops being a rule.** Scoped as `v0.139.0` items 1–2. **⚠️ TWO INSTRUMENT DEFECTS FOUND BY RUNNING THE READ:** the prompt fires only after a successful save (`:96-107`), so **8 of 9 vanished unrecorded**; and all 11 `DUE_CONCEPTS_DIGEST_LANDED` rows carry a **NULL `user_id`**, so this checkpoint's SECOND metric was **never computable**. **DISTAL `[CHECKPOINT — due 2026-11-09]` stands, and is now conditional on `v0.139.0` item 1 landing** — without it the November read is as blind as this one. **GATE CLEARED AND READ RUN 2026-08-11.** post-`v0.48.0`: **31 eligible, 0 returned, 0.00%**; pre-`v0.48.0` baseline 154/3/**1.95%** (sanity check passes against the known ~2.4%). **0% is underpowered, not negative:** expected returns at baseline for n=31 is 0.60, and P(zero \\ | 2026-09-10 | no change) = 54.3%, failing bootstrap clause 3 outright and reproducing exactly the decorative-checkpoint failure the `v0.48.0` digest-lift postmortem records. **Proximal (2026-09-10) — the funnel this release actually instrumented,** whose denominator is every learner finishing a first session and is therefore readable at current scale: `PROMPT_SHOWN → COMMITTED` rate, and `DIGEST_LANDED → FIRST_ANSWER_SUBMITTED` rate among committers. **Kill criterion:** if learners do not commit when asked, or committers do not act on the digest they asked for, the retention question never arises — the return-loop framing reverts to **unconfirmed** and is reopened rather than iterated on with further nudge tuning. **Distal (2026-11-09) — RE-SPECIFIED 2026-08-11, before it could fire against a broken instrument.** It originally read "the W1→W2 read itself." `v0.72.1`'s Query 5 then measured that the days 7–14 window sees only **3 of 11 real returners (27%)** — 4 return in days 2–7 before the window opens, 4 only after day 14. Left as written, this checkpoint would have fired in November measuring H1+H5 through an instrument that misses 73% of returns, and would likely have reported it ineffective regardless of what H1+H5 actually did. That is the decorative-checkpoint failure in its most damaging form: not a missing metric, but a confidently wrong one. **It is therefore now specified as the returned-after-day-7-unbounded read** (any activity after day 7, no upper bound), reported **alongside** the strict days 7–14 figure so the two definitions stay visible against each other and neither can be quoted alone. Cut by the exam-date segmentation defined in the Target-habit row below rather than as one blended percentage. **Denominator clause:** if the sample is still underpowered at that date, record "not yet measurable" and re-date it — that is not a verdict, and the proximal tier is what carries the kill criterion precisely so this one does not have to. **Dates and criteria proposed by Claude Code 2026-08-11, owner may override** — same precedent as the Challenge Quiz adoption row | 2026-08-11 |
| **Target-habit definition** (exam-date-relative return cadence vs. the current calendar-week W1→W2 boolean) | This session, 2026-07-28 — surfaced during a strategic reprioritization discussion (see "Prioritization Lens & Strategic Frame" above and the updated "Product Learning Loop" section at the bottom of this doc) | **Defined 2026-07-28 — retires the single blended W1→W2 boolean as the universal yardstick.** Segment by whether `UserEntity.examDate` is set (not by `profile_type` — a coarser proxy for the same thing; some non-`BOARD_EXAM` accounts may also set a real exam date). **(1) Exam-bound learners** (majority — `BOARD_EXAM` alone is 70.94% of profile-typed accounts, see the population-mix row above): natural arc is signup → sustained practice → sit the exam → legitimately stop. Metric: among users whose exam date has already passed, did they have ≥1 qualifying practice session in the 7 days immediately before that date? Users whose exam date hasn't arrived yet are `in_flight` — excluded from the scored denominator entirely (same convention the Diagnostic Read already uses for cohorts whose 14-day window hasn't closed), not scored as success or failure either way. Going quiet only *after* a user's own exam date is not churn — that's expected completion. **(2) Open-ended learners** (`STUDENT` and any account with no exam date set, ~27%+): no single terminal event — the existing W1→W2-style windowed read remains a reasonable frame here specifically; refine later against the learner's own `studyDaysPerWeek`, not urgent. **Does not excuse the existing 0/41 exam-dated-retention finding** — that measures disengagement *before* the goal, a real problem under either frame; the reframe only stops penalizing expected disengagement *after* the exam date. **Small-n caveat:** the scored group (exam date already passed) is likely very small right now — recent signups, PRC-clustered exam dates — a near-single-digit denominator means "not yet measurable," not a verdict, the same trap Round 1 already caught itself in with Query 2's 0/39. **Post-exam users still matter for other purposes** (referrals, retakes, Knowledge Impact creator supply) — not counting their post-exam quiet as retention failure doesn't mean they're uninteresting. **Application:** Round 2 (Diagnostic Read + H1+H5, due ~2026-08-06) should re-cut by this segmentation rather than one blended percentage — the query is a small job once run against real data then, not written now | **PARTIALLY ACTED ON in `v0.72.1`, 2026-08-11 — recorded here so this row does not read as untouched.** This row retired the blended W1→W2 boolean as the universal yardstick in *definition*; `v0.72.1` was the first release to change anything in *code* on the strength of it. Query 5 measured why it mattered: the days 7–14 boolean sees **3 of 11 real returners (27%)**, missing everyone who returns in days 2–7 or after day 14. The admin funnel now reports four windows side by side, with the strict figure preserved unchanged for comparability. **What this row still owes, and what was deliberately left:** the **exam-date segmentation** is NOT implemented — widening the window addresses the open-ended-learner half this row already called reasonable, while the exam-bound metric it defines (practice in the 7 days before a passed exam date) remains unbuilt, and this row's own small-n caveat still applies to it. Un-parked by that scored group becoming large enough to read | 2026-08-11 |
| Profile-type population mix across the full user base (STUDENT vs. BOARD_EXAM vs. other `ProfileType` values — not just the surge cohort) | Same reprioritization discussion, 2026-07-28 — needed to check whether "Review Set adoption is displacing note-authoring as the primary populate-path" holds product-wide or only for the `BOARD_EXAM`-only practice-first fast-path | **Run 2026-07-28 — answered, caveat does not apply.** `15-profile-type-population-mix.sql`, run against production: of profile-typed accounts, `BOARD_EXAM` is 70.94% (144) vs. `STUDENT` 27.09% (55) (Query B); `PROFESSIONAL`/`TEACHER` negligible. Not a surge artifact — pre-surge accounts were already 63.3%/34.0% `BOARD_EXAM`/`STUDENT` among profile-typed users (Query C), and the surge then pushed the post-surge window to 92.5%/7.5%. `BOARD_EXAM` has been the dominant segment since before the surge, not a surge-only skew — the `BOARD_EXAM`-only practice-first adopt-a-Review-Set fast-path reaches the majority segment, not a minority carve-out, so "Review Sets are becoming the real product" does not need the STUDENT-dominance caveat this row originally flagged. **Separate finding, not this row's original question but worth logging:** 40.1% of all accounts (136/339, Query A) and 27.4% of the surge-and-after window (20/73, Query C) have `profile_type` still NULL — corroborates, via an independent measurement path, the Diagnostic Read's existing ~50% onboarding non-completion finding (see the Diagnostic Read section above). A large minority of signups may never reach a profile-typed state at all, which bears on how the target-habit / retention reads above should be interpreted — a return-cadence habit can't be built by users who never finish onboarding in the first place | none — answered | 2026-07-28 |
| **`bg-card` Tailwind utility resolved to no-op** — 23 literal usages across 9 files (22 rendered elements) rendered as bordered-but-unfilled panels instead of the elevated-surface fill the code implies | Found 2026-07-30 while pressure-testing `v0.66.0`'s `ScoreReveal`-nesting concern (a `bg-card`-on-`bg-surface-alt` "card-in-a-card" worry that turned out to depend on whether `bg-card` even resolves) | **Shipped — v0.66.2.** No `--color-card`/`--card` token existed anywhere in `frontend/app/globals.css`'s `@theme inline` block, so Tailwind v4 silently dropped the unrecognized utility rather than erroring. `--card` registered aliased to `--background` (not `--surface-alt`) — chosen and verified via actual `getComputedStyle` checks, not just visual screenshot inspection: standalone nested panels (`ScoreReveal`, `public-flashcards-preview.tsx`) sit inside a parent `Card` (`bg-surface-alt`) and genuinely gain visible separation from `--background` (`rgb(255,255,255)` vs. the container's `rgb(249,250,251)`, confirmed by computed style, not eyeballed). Count corrected twice before shipping: 9 → 21 (missed `long-exam.tsx` ×8 and `challenge-quiz.tsx` ×5 on first re-check) → 23 literal occurrences / 9 files / 22 elements on a stricter recount (a Codex audit found the flashcard flip-button contributes 2 source-line occurrences for 1 rendered element). That same Codex audit also caught a real reasoning error: two call sites (`challenge-quiz/page.tsx:2090`, `long-exam/page.tsx:1090`) apply `bg-card` directly on a `Card` component alongside `Card`'s own default `bg-surface-alt`; the original scoping claimed this was a deliberate override the fix "preserved," but `cn()` has no `tailwind-merge` and the compiled stylesheet's cascade order (not JSX class order) decides equal-specificity ties, so `bg-surface-alt` wins regardless — confirmed via `getComputedStyle` returning an identical color for both an "override" `Card` and a plain `Card`. The token choice itself was unaffected (it rests on the nested-panel evidence, not the false override claim), but the claim was removed from `RELEASES.md` rather than left standing. One incidentally-verified genuine visual change: `quiz-generation-overlay.tsx`'s modal panel goes from a translucent blur-bleed-through card to a solid, legible dialog — checked in both themes, no regression | none — shipped | 2026-07-30 |
| **Messaging Architecture — "We sell the learning system, features support the promise"** | Owner + Claude Code discussion, 2026-07-31/2026-08-01, originating from Company Redefinition Phase 4 §4 item 4 (Pro exam-pass re-messaging) — widened after the initial Board-Exam-Mode-led drafts were reconsidered as narrowing NoteLib's identity back to an exam-review app. **Supersedes the "External marketing copy vs. internal 'learning OS' positioning" row this replaces** (2026-07-28) — that row's narrower landing-page-copy concern and its `[DECISION, then EVIDENCE]` conversion-test gate are both absorbed by this wider initiative | **Ratified 2026-08-01.** Core principle: *"We sell the learning system. Features simply support that promise."* (Spotify-Premium analogy: sell "Listen without interruptions," not offline playlists/bitrate/skips — NoteLib should sell "Always know what to learn next," not Board Exam Mode/Companion/Adaptive Practice/Review Sets/Progress individually). Product-wide scope, not a one-off paywall rewrite: landing page, pricing page, upgrade flows/paywall modals, checkout, marketing copy, and in-app upsells (e.g. Exam Hub, quota-limit modals) should all eventually reinforce the same hero and hierarchy rather than reinventing framing per surface; this principle extends to future product copy generally, not only monetization surfaces. **Hierarchy (locked):** Hero (universal emotional outcome, profile-agnostic) → Supporting paragraph (explains "the learning system," still profile-agnostic) → Profile-specific bullets (personalizes via the existing `ProfileType`-keyed copy-resolution pattern already used elsewhere in this codebase — e.g. `getCollectionLabels`, per-plan `adaptivePracticeMessage` — not a new mechanism) → Features (evidence supporting the promise; this is where Board Exam Mode, Companion, Adaptive Practice, Review Sets, Progress, Knowledge Impact, Explore each live — none of them become the hero anywhere). **Ratified hero/supporting:** "Always know what to learn next." / "NoteLib turns your notes into a complete learning system — organized, prioritized, and ready whenever you sit down to study." **Illustrative profile bullets (to be refined at implementation scoping, not final copy):** Student — stay organized across every subject · know what to study next · practice with confidence. Exam Taker — stay organized across your review · know what deserves attention before exam day · practice under real board-exam conditions with Board Exam Mode. Teacher — organize teaching materials into ready-to-use Review Sets · build better assessments faster · track your students' progress. Professional — **flagged aspirational, not shippable as real copy yet**: `PROFESSIONAL` is enum-only today with no dedicated feature set (per `CLAUDE.md`); illustrative bullets ("keep growing after graduation, one note at a time," "refresh what matters most before it's needed," "stay ready for certifications and recurring requirements") describe an experience that doesn't exist yet — do not wire this profile's bullets live until Professional has real capability behind it. **Plus/Pro naming:** product names stay unchanged everywhere (checkout, Settings, badges, receipts, support) — avoids the bifurcated-vocabulary risk this project already identified and avoided one release earlier, when Creator/Curated Learning labels were deliberately kept internal-frame-only rather than user-facing (Company Redefinition Phase 4, ratified 2026-07-31). Only the aspirational tagline changes: `plans.ts`'s existing `PRO.title` field (currently "Best for exam prep") and `PLUS`'s equivalent become outcome-framed — e.g. Plus → "Guided learning built around your notes," Pro → "Your complete learning system" — `name: "Pro"` / `name: "Plus"` untouched. **Deliberately not gated behind a pre-launch conversion experiment** — an explicit owner call distinguishing this from pricing/checkout *mechanics* changes (which still need evidence): narrative-consistency-with-product-vision is treated as a product design decision, not an optimization experiment. Conversion is still measured post-launch and iterated on if it regresses; it just doesn't block initial rollout. **Second slice shipped as `v0.76.0` (2026-08-14) — the money surfaces.** `FREE.title` (the item this row named as explicitly owed) is now *"Start with ready-made study material"*, the `PLAN_COMPARISON_ROWS` "Best for" row describes learner situations, and the paywall headline/body carry the promise. **Two rulings from that slice bind future ones:** upgrade **button** labels stay feature-named (a button says what the click does), and `PLAN_CARD_SUBTEXT` must describe the tier rather than a feature, because it is keyed on plan type alone and renders on every paywall. `FREE.description` was deliberately left factual. **Still un-actioned: the landing page, the Exam Hub upsell, per-plan feature bullets, and both `TEACHER_*` paywalls** — the last of these needs a teacher-positioning scoping pass that has not happened. **First slice shipped in `v0.68.0` (2026-08-01)** — this row was written before that merged and originally read "Not yet implemented anywhere." The `/pricing` hero and supporting paragraph shipped the ratified copy **word for word** ("Always know what to learn next." / "NoteLib turns your notes into a complete learning system — organized, prioritized, and ready whenever you sit down to study."), along with the `<meta description>`, the second-section heading, and the exact `PLUS`/`PRO` taglines this row ratifies ("Guided learning built around your notes" / "Your complete learning system"), with `name: "Plus"`/`name: "Pro"` untouched as specified. `FREE.description` was also rebalanced at signoff — for card-alignment reasons, not positioning, and deliberately kept a factual feature list so it makes no tier-boundary claim against the ratified `FREE=adopt` placement. **`FREE.title` is explicitly still owed to this initiative:** an outcome-framed candidate was written during `v0.68.0` and reverted, because deriving it from consistency with `PLUS`/`PRO` alone contradicted `FREE=adopt` — it needs writing against the hierarchy above, not against its siblings. **Every remaining surface is unimplemented** (paywall modal, Exam Hub upsell, landing page, `PLAN_COMPARISON_ROWS`'s "Best for" row, per-plan feature bullets) | **No gate on the ratified architecture** (owner decision, not awaiting evidence) — and explicitly **not** gated on a pre-launch conversion experiment, per the owner call recorded in Status. Each remaining surface needs its own scoping pass and its own `/kickoff` before code changes; this is explicitly incremental, not a single release. `FREE.title` is the one already-identified concrete item, and must be written against the locked hierarchy rather than derived from `PLUS`/`PRO` consistency | 2026-08-01 |
| **`ONBOARDING_V2_ABANDONED` instrumentation bug** (over-fires on every onboarding step transition; several early-return redirects leak the event with no matching `ONBOARDING_V2_STARTED`) | Found investigating the "Funnel re-check" anomaly above (`ABANDONED` distinct-users 69 > `STARTED` distinct-users 68) — root cause verified by direct code read (`frontend/app/onboarding/page.tsx`) plus an independent Opus pressure-test that read the same code and refined the analysis (found the leak-path mechanism the initial over-fire theory alone didn't explain) | **~~In progress — sent to Codex 2026-07-28~~ — LANDED, verified in code 2026-08-19.** Both bugs are fixed in `app/onboarding/page.tsx`: the abandonment cleanup now carries `[]` deps with a `currentStepRef` mirror (so it fires on true unmount only, not on every step transition), and the shared invariant `!startedTrackedRef.current \|\| !shouldTrackAbandonmentRef.current` guards the emit, which is the single-guard shape the prompt specified rather than the per-site patching that caused the bug. **⚠️ The row sat at "in progress" for three weeks after the work shipped** — second stale row found in the same 2026-08-19 pass. **Still genuinely open:** real abandonment (tab close / hard navigation) is still never captured — no `pagehide`/`beforeunload`/`visibilitychange` handler exists — and the discriminating query `16-abandoned-leak-path-check.sql` is still unrun. Original text follows. **In progress — fix scoped, sent to Codex 2026-07-28.** Prompt: `docs/codex-prompts/v0.61.0-onboarding-abandoned-event-fix.md`. Two compounding bugs: (1) the abandonment cleanup is keyed on `[draft.currentStep]`, so it fires on nearly every step transition, not just true unmount; (2) several early-return redirects (already-verified-email, two already-completed-onboarding checks, a `getMe()` rejection) never clear the abandonment guard and never reach `STARTED` either, leaking `ABANDONED` with no matching `STARTED`. Fix: decouple the cleanup from `draft.currentStep` via a ref, and add one shared invariant (`!startedTrackedRef.current \|\| !shouldTrackAbandonmentRef.current`) rather than patching each leak site — the per-site approach is what produced the bug (two adjacent branches on the same condition, only one clearing the guard). **Separately, real abandonment (tab close/hard navigation) is currently never captured at all** — no `pagehide`/`beforeunload`/`visibilitychange` handler exists; that's a distinct, larger, not-yet-scoped gap, intentionally out of scope for this fix. Discriminating query available, not yet run: `16-abandoned-leak-path-check.sql` | Codex delivery, then Claude Code audit-diff before commit (per this doc's standard Codex-delivery process) | 2026-07-28 |
| **Authoring by inference — evolve the note authoring UX from manual classification to inferred metadata with explicit override** | Owner reframing 2026-08-04, after pressure-testing the "should a note have a Learner Level?" question with a product-UX GPT (`14-learner-level-necessity-gpt-consultation.md`). **Recorded as a direction in `ADR-001` rather than only here**, because it constrains what any future authoring surface may do | **SHIPPED as `v0.75.0`, signed off 2026-08-14.** Depth only — Domain Context stays uninferred, and the `Authored Depth` rename shipped copy-only with the column unchanged. **One binding narrowing found at the pressure test: the profile pre-fill applies to CURATORS ONLY.** It initially fired for every profile while the control renders for Teacher/Admin alone, so learners silently persisted an invisible, unclearable depth — constraint (b)'s safety argument is that a human *saw* the value, which a hidden field cannot satisfy. Learners therefore get no depth pre-fill and keep resolving through the profile fallback. **Still open from this row, deliberately: the Review Set leg fires only on bulk-generate**, because that is where bulk authoring happens; single-note creation still has no Review Set context. **The four constraints below remain binding on any future authoring surface.** Historical direction follows. The four-axis knowledge model is unchanged and confirmed: depth is a property of the *content*, since Grade School / Senior High / College Engineering / Board Review Algebra are different knowledge artifacts rather than one artifact with four quiz settings. Two placements were considered and rejected on the record — depth on the *reader* alone (makes a College-profile learner get a college quiz on a grade-school note) and depth on the *program* via `course_programs.learner_level_id` (Civil Engineering spans Year 1–4 plus Board Review, Nursing spans College plus Board Review, so one program has many depths and normalising just relocates the coupling). What changes is authoring: **Review Set → author profile → explicit override.** Four constraints are binding and recorded in the ADR: **(a)** Subject and Domain Context are **not** valid depth-inference sources — `Algebra` spans every depth, and inferring depth from Domain Context re-couples the two axes the release just separated; **(b)** inference is a **UI pre-fill only, never a server-side default write**, because `domain_context IS NULL` is the promotion-backlog marker and a default would silently destroy it; **(c)** year-level granularity (CE Year 1–4) is **out of scope** — `COLLEGE` stays a single value; **(d)** the admin label may be renamed (`Educational Level` / `Authored Depth`) but **not** to `Intended Audience`, which `notes.target_profile_type` already occupies in the editor | **GATE FULLY CLEARED — verified at the `v0.75.0` kickoff, 2026-08-13, and KICKED OFF as `v0.75.0` the same day.** Both sequenced prerequisites were already satisfied, and this row had gone stale in a way that made unblocked work look blocked. **First prerequisite — "make authoring metadata editable on `STUDY_PACK_READY` notes" — SHIPPED IN `v0.70.0`, four releases before this row was last reviewed.** The claim that *"the editor closes once a Study Pack exists"* is false: `AGENTS.md:1081` records that on a `STUDY_PACK_READY` note, Edit stays on Note Detail and Teacher/Admin authors may edit Target Audience, Domain Context, and Note Learner Level; `private-note-detail-page-client.tsx:1445-1452` opens that inline metadata editor for any non-draft note. **Second prerequisite — R4 — RESOLVED 2026-08-04** (`ADR-001:266`), passing on all three steps. **The same staleness sits in `ADR-001`'s own "Sequencing — gated on R4" subsection and is corrected there in this release (item 6), which matters more:** an ADR outranks a feature doc, so a stale gate inside one blocks work that is actually unblocked. **Scope note carried into the release: DEPTH ONLY.** `ADR-001` constraint 1 authorizes an inference chain for `learner_level` alone and names **no** source for Domain Context, which is also the field where a pre-fill is most dangerous — `domain_context IS NULL` is the promotion-backlog marker, so a pre-filled select a curator tabs past silently converts an observable backlog row into a decided one. **Leg 1 needs building, not just wiring:** `NoteCollectionEntity` carries no `learnerLevel`, no plan-template entity carries one, and no authoring surface knows its target Review Set — so the ADR's "a CE Board Review set is board-depth by construction" is a reading of a title, not a stored fact | 2026-08-13 |
| **R4 — does a broader Domain Context degrade generated content?** (post-deploy verification owed by `v0.69.0`) | `01` risk R4, restated in `RELEASES.md` v0.69.0 under "Verification owed." The concern: a Domain Context is often *broader* than the `course_program` it replaces (`Engineering Mathematics` vs `Civil Engineering`), and a vaguer domain constraint can make generated content drift generic. No test detects this — the prompt-building tests assert which values reach the model, never whether the output is good | **RESOLVED 2026-08-04 — passed on all three steps, run against production once `v0.70.0` deployed.** The 8-value Domain Context set is **not** amended. A `Strength of Materials` note set to the deliberately broader `Engineering Sciences` kept every domain marker (`hoop (circumferential)` / `longitudinal` stress, `Lame’s equations`, the `ASME Boiler and Pressure Vessel Code`, the thin/thick-wall classification, its civil-engineering framing) — zero of five drift checks fired, verified single-variable in production afterwards. Control regenerated near-identically; level precedence held (a `BOARD_EXAM_REVIEW` note read by a `COLLEGE` profile produced board-level multi-step items). Runbook and rubric: `17-r4-verification-runbook.md`. Recorded in ADR-001 under “R4 verification” | **Gate cleared. Bulk authoring is unblocked** — the constraint that survived three releases is satisfied, not merely restated. **Does NOT settle applicability**: R4 validated the Domain Context *value set*, not which programs share which subjects. The `06`/`08` groupings remain unverified against current PRC board syllabi and are a `[DECISION]`/`[EFFORT]` gate on Release B’s family-expansion defaults | 2026-08-04 |
